# Documentation refresh: working notes

Status: drafting source of truth for the 2026-09 documentation refresh. This
file is not published. It is removed from the branch before the pull request
is opened; the commit that added it keeps it recoverable from git history.

Snapshot: `main` at `38eae99` (2026-09-23), clean working tree. Facts were
gathered by reading source, tests, fixtures, and goldens. Nothing was crawled,
no model was pulled, and no package was installed. "Verified" means read in the
cited file unless a note says a command was run.

## 1. Decisions made while drafting

1. **Branch.** The session is pinned to `claude/intelligent-clarke-9wodjp`, so
   work happens there instead of `docs/refresh-{date}`.
2. **Pages source.** The public site is generated by `site/build.py` into
   `site/**/index.html`; `.github/workflows/pages.yml:30` publishes the
   committed HTML without rebuilding. Page copy is edited in `site/build.py`
   and the HTML is regenerated with `python3 site/build.py` (PYTHONPATH=src),
   which is what `make site` runs (`Makefile:16-17`).
3. **No new site pages.** `tests/unit/test_site_build.py:32-44` pins the exact
   page list, and tests are out of scope. So the glossary and "Reading your
   report" live in `docs/`, and the existing `coverage` page (already titled
   "What Axcess checks", `site/build.py:74-79`) carries the checks table.
4. **One glossary.** `docs/glossary.md` is the single source. The site FAQ
   glossary (`site/build.py:1359-1445`) is replaced by a rendering of
   `docs/glossary.md`, so the two cannot disagree.
5. **Files that must not move.** Code or tests cite these paths:
   `docs/hosting.md` (`Makefile:55,60`, `protected_auth.py:10`,
   `tests/ui/test_routes.py:1619`), `docs/coverage-tracker.md`
   (`Tracking.tsx:334` shows it in the UI), `docs/protected-scans.md`
   (`server.py:1237`), `docs/architecture.md` (`fonts.css:8`),
   `DETECTION_EFFICACY.md` (CI path filter, `detection-evals.yml:7,25`).
   Where one of these moves, a short pointer stays at the old path.
6. **Report group names.** The Issues table labels are canonical:
   Barrier, Needs review, Informational (`Issues.tsx:806-812`). The brief
   expected "Needs manual review"; that string does not exist in the product.

## 3. Open questions (running list)

1. **"Zero false positive goal."** No such goal is written anywhere in the
   repo. The documented target is a false discovery rate below 5% on a
   synthetic labeled corpus (`tests/quality/README.md:12-16`,
   `DETECTION_EFFICACY.md:12`), and that gate scores frozen labels without
   running any detector (`src/audit/quality_benchmark.py:8-12`). The glossary
   states the goal as scoped to the Barrier group. Confirm the wording.
2. **Report group names.** The review lane has six names in the product
   ("Needs review", "Needs confirmation", "Review leads", "Expert Review",
   "expert review", "Review-only"). The brief expected "Needs manual review".
   Pick one.
3. **"What to fix" vs "User impact".** "What to fix" exists only in the
   workbook's per-issue Instances table and More Issues sheet
   (`xlsx_export.py:160,173`) and repeats the issue's fix steps on every row.
   No "User impact" column exists anywhere; the nearest field is the workbook
   issue tab's "Impact" row (`xlsx_export.py:661`), filled from "why it
   matters". Renaming is a code change and out of scope here.
4. **"Internal apps."** Login scans refuse any host that resolves to a
   private or loopback address (`src/audit/protected/egress.py:778-782`,
   `session.py:149`). Campus sites on public address space pass. Public
   (no-login) scans have no such check. The brief's "internal apps" framing
   was narrowed to "sites behind a login, including SSO and two-factor".
5. **Barrier vs "act without confirmation."** The dashboard says barrier groups
   can be acted on "without confirmation" (`Dashboard.tsx:115-116,137`), while
   the Alfa text asks for confirmation and final exports require an expert
   decision on every barrier (`export_readiness.py:86-128`).
6. **Priority hides single-page severity.** Any issue on one page shows "Low"
   priority, even when critical (`issues.py:1188-1199`), and the Issues table
   has no severity column.

7. **Comparison items to re-check by hand** (vendor pages were blocked here):
   whether Siteimprove still reports SIA-R83 "Text is clipped when resized";
   the exact wording of Siteimprove help article 80000448387 on crawling sites
   with multi-factor sign-in; whether the free axe DevTools tier can switch to
   the WCAG 2.2 rule set; whether Siteimprove still has a "Link text is too
   generic" check. See Appendix G, "Open items to verify by hand".
8. **LSA manual testing links.** `accessibility.lsa.umich.edu` and
   `accessibility.umich.edu` were blocked here, so the linked pages were found
   by search only: LSA Web Accessibility resources
   (https://accessibility.lsa.umich.edu/browse-resources/web-accessibility.html)
   and LSA Accessibility training
   (https://accessibility.lsa.umich.edu/learn/training.html). Confirm these
   are the right destinations.

#### 5. Term definitions to replace with the new glossary

| Term | Location | Current definition (short quote) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| barrier / likely barrier | site/build.py:600-603 | Lane "Likely barrier": "Deterministic rule failures ... High confidence, though a person still verifies the fix." |
| barrier | site/build.py:597 | "a lead can never silently become a confirmed barrier" |
| barrier | README.md:188-189 | "Informational evidence cannot silently become a barrier." |
| barrier | README.md:260-261 | group can be "accepted, rejected as a false positive, remediated, or retained as an open barrier with rationale" |
| likely barrier / review lead | DETECTION_EFFICACY.md:4-6 | "Deterministic engines may produce likely-barrier findings. Browser probes, OCR/VLM, and semantic analyzers produce review leads" |
| likely barrier / review lead | tests/quality/README.md:25-29 | axe and Alfa `failed` "may enter the likely-barrier lane"; `cantTell`, probes, OCR/VLM, semantic "must remain expert-review leads" |
| barrier (comparison) | site/build.py:644-646 | New: "Barriers seen now"; Resolved: "no longer observed under the same scope and checks"; Still open: "present in both scans" |
| review lead | site/build.py:605-608 | Lane "Review lead": "Browser-observed behaviour ... The evidence is real; the interpretation needs an expert." |
| review lead | site/build.py:1377-1379 (glossary) | "A result that needs a person to confirm it before it counts, such as a keyboard-trap observation or an AI suggestion." Conflicts with build.py:610-613, which puts AI suggestions in a separate "Needs confirmation" lane |
| review lead | docs/coverage-tracker.md:46-47 | "AI-assisted: a local model creates a review lead. It is never treated as a conformance verdict." |
| needs confirmation | site/build.py:610-613 | Lane "Needs confirmation": "AI-assisted suggestions ... always confirmed or rejected by a person before they count." |
| needs confirmation | README.md:188-189 | "AI-assisted results and Alfa `cantTell` outcomes are explicitly marked as needing confirmation." |
| needs confirmation | README.md:108; AGENTS.md:35 | "Local-LLM lead requiring expert confirmation"; "a lead requiring human confirmation" |
| AI-assisted lead | site/build.py:340-343 | "A local AI model flags likely problems as leads. A person confirms each one before it counts." |
| needs review / expert review | README.md:22 | "Axcess produces accessibility evidence for expert review." |
| expert review | DETECTION_EFFICACY.md:41-42 | "keeps model- and probe-backed results in the expert review lane" |
| expert review | site/build.py:769 | occurrences not through expert review "are *detected*, not *confirmed*" |
| informational (evidence lane) | README.md:255-256 | "deterministic failures, behavioral observations, AI leads, and informational evidence use separate lanes" (a fourth lane; site shows only three, build.py:599-615) |
| informational (VLM class) | image-text-audit-prompt.md:88; PLAN.md:182; docs/architecture.md:218; docs/system-design-and-coverage-gap.md:163 | VLM label `informational_text` / weight "informational=3". Same word, unrelated meaning |
| finding | docs/README.md:141-142 | "Finding: the review unit: one image, one scan, with severity, priority, remediation hint, and a workflow status." (image-only) |
| finding / issue | image-text-audit-prompt.md:55 | "the same image reused across 40 pages is one finding with 40 occurrences" |
| accessibility report / issue | AGENTS.md:37-41 | report = "one completed `scans` row and its child evidence"; issue = "a grouped view within that report: one axe/probe/semantic rule or one `(classification, alt_adequacy)` image finding group" |
| issue group / occurrence | site/build.py:1325-1326 (FAQ) | occurrence "one problem on one page"; issue group "collects occurrences that share a cause and probably share a fix" |
| issue group | site/build.py:1373-1375 | "Occurrences that share a cause and probably share a fix, presented as one row in the issue table." |
| occurrence | site/build.py:1376 | "One instance of a problem on one page, or in one DOM state." |
| observation / occurrence / issue | whitepaper/AXCESS-WHITE-PAPER.md:121 | "An observation is an immutable record of what a method saw. An occurrence is that result at one target and state. An issue groups occurrences that probably share a fix." |
| issue group | README.md:173-175 | "groups repeated occurrences into actionable issue groups ... 19 issue groups / 965 detected occurrences" |
| evidence | site/build.py:1369-1370 | "What a check actually observed: the page, the element, a snippet, a screenshot, the rule, and the method." |
| false positive | site/build.py:1382-1383 | "A result the reviewer has judged not to be a real barrier." |
| severity (image) | PLAN.md:128, 187; docs/README.md:143-145; docs/architecture.md:225-228, 282 | enum `critical/major/minor/info`; score >=8 critical, >=5 major, >=2 minor, else info (matches src/audit/synthesizer/priority.py:12-14,69) |
| severity | docs/design-principles.md:42 | "Severity is critical/major/minor/info, Jira-compatible vocabulary." |
| severity to Jira priority | docs/user-guide.md:108 | "Priority (critical to Highest, info to Lowest)" |
| impact | (no doc definition) | axe impact scale critical/serious/moderate/minor is only in code (src/audit/web/issues.py:99-107, image severities mapped onto it). Loosely used: "ranked by impact on people" (site/build.py:1009), "sorted by impact" (site/build.py:1272), "priority mapped from severity/impact" (docs/system-design-and-coverage-gap.md:419) |
| priority (image score) | docs/README.md:143-145; docs/architecture.md:215-224; PLAN.md:179-186; docs/developer-guide.md:159-169 | classification weight + alt adequacy weight + log1p(occurrences) + above-fold bonus |
| priority (issue) | docs/system-design-and-coverage-gap.md:396 | "Priority (severity x log(1+pages), pinned in `web/issues.py`)"; correct (src/audit/web/issues.py:1188-1199) |
| priority (future) | whitepaper/AXCESS-WHITE-PAPER.md:146 | "transparent reasons for priority: user impact, essential-task risk, prevalence, confidence, regression status, and fix leverage" |

## Appendix B: Report groups, severity, exports, revealed-by, report screens

Source file: agent notes `lanes_exports.md`, copied verbatim with headings demoted.

### Axcess verified facts: report groups, severity, exports, "revealed by", report screens

Repo: /home/user/axcess at commit 38eae99 (2026-09-23), clean working tree (`git status --short` empty).
Method: read source and golden files only. No tests, servers, crawls, or builds were run.
All paths are repo-relative. `path:line` or `path:start-end`.
"VERIFIED" = read in code/golden. "UNVERIFIED" = stated with reason.

---

#### A. Report groups (review lanes)

##### A.0 Definition

- Type: `ReviewLane = Literal["likely_barrier", "expert_review", "informational"]` (src/audit/web/issues.py:67). Confidence: `EvidenceConfidence = Literal["high","medium","low"]` (issues.py:68).
- Stated intent (issues.py:60-66): "deterministic rule failures may enter the remediation worklist, ambiguous/AI-assisted observations must be confirmed by an expert, and non-problems stay visible only as informational evidence."
- Every `IssueRow` carries `review_lane`, `evidence_confidence`, `evidence_summary`, `high_confidence_occurrence_count` (issues.py:228-234).
- Frontend mirror: `ReviewLane`, `EvidenceConfidence` (src/audit/web/frontend/src/api/types.ts:682-683).
- Lane is computed only from pipeline, rule id, Alfa outcome, and image classification/adequacy. Finding status never changes the lane (no status reference in the lane code, issues.py:764-952, 1027-1057). Status does change where a row lands in the audit report and workbook (see A.4).

##### A.1 Exact lane rule per source

Group identity: DOM findings group by `(pipeline, rule_id, outcome_group)` where outcome_group is set only for Alfa (`failed` / `cant_tell`) (src/audit/web/a11y_queries.py:358-363). Image findings group by `(classification, alt_adequacy)` (issues.py:1014-1021).

Default for every DOM group before pipeline branches: `expert_review`, `medium`, summary "Observed evidence requires expert confirmation." (issues.py:764-767).

| Source (pipeline / rule) | Condition | Lane | Confidence | evidence_summary (exact) | Cite |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| axe-core (`pipeline='axe'`) | any stored axe violation, any impact, any level incl. best practice | likely_barrier | high | "Deterministic axe-core rule failure; verify after remediation." | issues.py:946-952 |
| Interaction probe (click-revealed) | stored as ordinary `pipeline='axe'` rows with `revealed_by`; no own pipeline or lane | likely_barrier (same as axe) | high | same as axe | src/audit/analyzer/interaction/base.py:7-15; src/audit/crawler/orchestrator.py:1868-1888 |
| Configured search pass | also `RevealedViolation(..., "Configured search")` on axe path | likely_barrier | high | same as axe | src/audit/crawler/search.py:155 |
| Siteimprove Alfa, outcome `failed` | issue key `alfa:<rule>:failed` | likely_barrier | high; high_confidence count = failed count | "Alfa produced {n} failed ACT occurrence(s). Observed: {diagnostics or 'the stored rule requirements failed.'}" | issues.py:881-883, 896-907 |
| Siteimprove Alfa, outcome `cant_tell` | issue key `alfa:<rule>:cant_tell` | expert_review | medium | "Alfa returned {n} cantTell occurrence(s); this is not a failure. {diagnostics or 'Review the stored target in page context.'}" | issues.py:887-895 |
| Semantic (LLM) analyzers | `pipeline='semantic'`, key `semantic:<sc>` | expert_review | medium | "AI-assisted semantic lead; confirm in page context." | issues.py:768-772 |
| Keyboard probe, current | not legacy (see next row) | expert_review | medium | "Measured Tab and Shift+Tab exit attempts both remained on the same observable element; manually check for another documented exit command." | issues.py:823-828 |
| Keyboard probe, legacy | rule `keyboard-trap-modal-no-escape` or `keyboard-trap-iframe`, or `keyboard-trap-stuck` whose summaries lack "Shift+Tab attempts" | informational | low | "An older Axcess probe used a one-direction, Escape, or iframe heuristic that does not establish WCAG 2.1.2. Retained for audit history; do not report it as a barrier without manual evidence." | issues.py:708-717, 799-822 |
| Responsive / zoom probe | any | expert_review | medium | "Browser geometry signal; confirm at 320 CSS px and 200% zoom." | issues.py:829-832 |
| Focus probe | any | expert_review | medium | "Browser focus probe lead; confirm across the full interaction state." | issues.py:833-838 |
| Visual probe, `visual-autoplay-audio-no-control` | any | expert_review | medium | "Runtime playback advanced while audible audio had no detected native or explicitly associated custom control; confirm the interaction manually." | issues.py:864-869 |
| Visual probe, `visual-motion-no-pause` with runtime measurement | summary contains "Runtime playback measurement:" | expert_review | medium | "Runtime motion or marquee evidence requires expert confirmation of duration, page context, and any custom controls." | issues.py:870-875 |
| Visual probe, `visual-motion-no-pause` legacy | no summary contains "Runtime playback measurement:" | informational | low | "An older detector saw autoplay markup but did not establish that media loaded, played, or met the WCAG duration threshold. Retained for audit history; do not report it as a barrier without manual evidence." | issues.py:718-725, 841-863 |
| Visual probe, other rules (visual order) | any | expert_review | medium | "Visual-model lead; compare DOM and visual reading order manually." | issues.py:876-880 |
| Protected image analysis (`protected_image`) | any | expert_review | medium | "Local image-analysis lead; no conformance decision was automated." | issues.py:773-796 |
| Image-of-text (OCR/VLM), alt_adequacy = `adequate` | any classification, incl. unclassified | informational | low if unclassified, else medium | "Alt comparison appears adequate; retained as non-actionable evidence." | issues.py:1027-1029, 1044-1049 |
| Image-of-text, classification missing (`unclassified`), adequacy not adequate | | expert_review | low | "Image analysis was inconclusive; classify manually before reporting a barrier." | issues.py:1019, 1028, 1045, 1050-1053 |
| Image-of-text, classified (essential / informational / logo / decorative / no_meaningful_text), adequacy missing / inadequate / partial / unknown | | expert_review | medium | "OCR/VLM-assisted image lead; confirm purpose and alternative in context." | issues.py:1044-1045, 1054-1057 |

Summary of the rule: only two sources ever produce `likely_barrier`: axe violations (incl. click-revealed and search-revealed) and Alfa `failed`. Only two produce `informational`: image groups whose alt is `adequate`, and legacy keyboard/visual observations. Everything else is `expert_review`. VERIFIED (issues.py:764-952, 1044).

Side effects tied to lane or legacy status:
- Legacy keyboard/visual rows are forced to `wcag_sc=None`, conformance `BP`, impact `minor`, YAML card dropped (issues.py:739-743, 748-757).
- Image `wcag_sc` is dropped for adequate or unclassified groups (issues.py:1029). Image `high_confidence_occurrence_count` is always 0 (issues.py:1081).
- Alfa rows never borrow an axe YAML card (issues.py:729-732, 479-482).
- Only axe violations are stored from axe (`violations` only; axe "incomplete" is not kept) (src/audit/analyzer/axe.py:288-297).
- Alfa `why_matters` text, failed: "A failed ACT outcome is strong automated evidence, not a conformance verdict. Confirm that the rule applies and reproduce the barrier before presenting the row as a confirmed accessibility issue." (issues.py:925-929). cantTell: "A cantTell outcome is not a failure. Review the target in context and record a human decision before describing it as a barrier." (issues.py:920-923).
- Protected image why_matters: "This is an image-analysis lead, not a conformance verdict. ..." (issues.py:781-784).

##### A.2 Lane mechanics (sort, filter, counts, export gating)

|---|---|---|
| Default sort | lane first (barrier, then expert_review, then informational), then priority desc; score never orders across lanes | issues.py:1220-1240; Issues.tsx:357, 395-419 |

| Overview rejection rate | excludes informational rows | ScanDetail.tsx:169-190 |

##### A.4 How lanes map into the audit report and workbook buckets

`_bucket_rows_into_cards` (audit_report.py:313-389), used by both the Markdown audit report and the xlsx Summary/hotspots/who's-affected (xlsx_export.py:1393):
1. Every backing finding triaged (remediated/accepted_risk/false_positive) -> Appendix A ("Already triaged: ...") (audit_report.py:338-347, 1506-1513).
2. Partly triaged -> Appendix A receipt "Triaged subset: ..." plus the open remainder continues (349-360).
3. `expert_review`: the `in_progress` subset becomes an issue card (expert-confirmed barrier); the `new`/`reviewing` subset goes to Appendix B (362-372).
4. `likely_barrier` open subset with a WCAG SC and not BP -> issue card (377-387).
5. `likely_barrier` that is BP or has no SC, and all `informational` -> Appendix B (382-384).
Cards sorted by severity rank then affected pages (388-389).

##### A.5 Inconsistencies found (lanes)

1. expert_review has at least six names: "Needs review" (Issues.tsx:810), "Needs confirmation" (IssueEvidence.tsx:59), "Review leads" (Dashboard.tsx:140), "Expert Review" (xlsx_export.py:127), "expert review" (audit_report.py:1337), "Review-only" (xlsx_export.py:970).
2. informational is "Informational" (Issues.tsx:811) vs "Informational evidence" (IssueEvidence.tsx:60).
3. likely_barrier is "Barrier"/"Barriers" in the UI (Issues.tsx:808; IssueEvidence.tsx:57; Dashboard.tsx:135) but "Likely Barrier"/"Likely-barrier"/"likely barrier" in exports (xlsx_export.py:127, 968; audit_report.py:1337).
4. Dashboard says barrier groups can be acted on "without confirmation" (Dashboard.tsx:115-116, 137), but the axe summary says "verify after remediation" (issues.py:952), the Alfa failed text says confirm and reproduce before presenting as confirmed (issues.py:925-929), and a final export is blocked until every likely_barrier finding has an expert disposition (export_readiness.py:86-128).
5. Workbook guidance tells readers to "Start with rows whose Evidence decision is Likely Barrier" (xlsx_export.py:126-127) but the Issues Overview has no such column. Actual headers: ID, Issue, Severity, Conformance Level, Remediation Ownership, Status, Instances, Pages, Details (xlsx_export.py:141-151; golden tests/unit/golden/rich_scan.xlsx.json row 8, A8 to I8). Issues Overview lists every lane (built from `issues.list_issues`, xlsx_export.py:426).
6. Workbook Summary "Likely-barrier issue groups" counts audit cards, which include expert-confirmed (`in_progress`) expert_review subsets and exclude likely_barrier rows that are BP or lack an SC (audit_report.py:362-387). It will not match the UI "Barrier" count.
7. Audit report Appendix B is titled "Review leads and informational evidence" (audit_report.py:1310) but also contains likely_barrier rows without an SC, e.g. golden lines "**likely barrier / high confidence**" for `page-has-heading-one` and `region` (tests/unit/golden/rich_scan.audit.md:1866-1867).
8. Informational image findings still become Jira "Bug" tickets: golden row "WCAG 1.4.5: image of text, Acme Corp" (logo, adequate alt), Priority "Lowest", label `class-logo` (tests/unit/golden/rich_scan.jira.csv:38-50). The audit report lists the same group as "informational / medium confidence" (rich_scan.audit.md:1874).
9. Legacy informational keyboard/visual rows are labeled "Needs expert review (observed lead)" in Markdown inventory and Jira (markdown_report.py:239-240; jira_export.py:123-124).
10. In the Issues table, "Barrier" and "Needs review" tags share the same "flag" styling; only text differs (Issues.tsx:619, 787-799). Informational uses a neutral tag.

---

#### B. Severity, impact, priority, conformance, status

##### B.1 Severity/impact vocabularies and their sources

| Source | Values emitted | Cite |
|---|---|---|
| axe-core impact | critical, serious, moderate, minor, or None (anything else coerced to None) | src/audit/analyzer/axe.py:195, 315-317 |
| Group impact for a DOM issue row | impact of the first finding row in the group (rows ordered by pipeline, rule_id, page URL); not a max | a11y_queries.py:348-350, 365-370 |
| Siteimprove Alfa | always None ("Alfa's ACT outcomes intentionally do not provide axe-style impact") | src/audit/analyzer/alfa.py:85-90 |
| Keyboard probe | "critical" for the measured Tab/Shift+Tab trap (the only check `run()` calls). Modal-escape (critical) and untitled-iframe (serious) checks still exist in code but are not called; rule ids marked "legacy; no longer emitted" | keyboard/probe.py:97-109, 184, 334-335, 419-422; keyboard/base.py:18-20, 44 |
| Responsive probe | "serious" | responsive/probe.py:244, 272, 310; responsive/base.py:41 |
| Focus probe | "serious" (default) | focus/base.py:41 |
| Visual probe | default "serious"; visual-order model confidence high/medium/low -> serious/moderate/minor | visual/base.py:35; visual/probe.py:252-254 |
| Semantic analyzers | model confidence high/medium/low -> serious/moderate/minor | e.g. semantic/analyzers/sc_1_2_1.py:123, sc_2_4_6.py:123, sc_3_3_2.py:120 |
| Legacy keyboard/visual rows | forced "minor" | issues.py:748-757 |
| Image-of-text severity | critical (score >= 8), major (>= 5), minor (>= 2), info (else) | src/audit/synthesizer/priority.py:12-15, 30-34, 69-77 |
| Image priority_score | classification weight (essential 4, informational 3, logo 1, decorative 1, no_meaningful_text 0, none 0) + adequacy weight (missing 3, inadequate 2, partial 1, adequate 0) + ln(1 + occurrences) + 1 if above fold | priority.py:5-10, 37-66 |
| Image issue row impact | worst severity across the group | issues.py:1036, 1071-1072 |

Mappings applied downstream:

| Where | Mapping | Cite |
|---|---|---|
| Issue priority weight `_SEVERITY_WEIGHT` | critical 4.0, serious 3.0, moderate 2.0, minor 1.0, major 3.0, info 1.0; missing/None -> 1.0 | issues.py:99-108, 1198 |
| Audit report and workbook severity word `_IMPACT_SEVERITY` | critical->Critical, serious->Serious, moderate->Moderate, minor->Minor, major->Serious, info->Minor; missing/None -> "Moderate" | audit_report.py:91-98, 1364; xlsx_export.py:331-332 |
| Audit report severity reason | >50 pages: "Single issue across **N page(s)** ... one fix has big payoff."; Critical: "Completely blocks an assistive-technology user from the affected content, no workaround."; Serious: "A real barrier for affected users, even if a workaround sometimes exists."; else "{n} finding(s) on {p} page(s)." | audit_report.py:1543-1558 |
| Jira Priority | image: critical Highest, major High, minor Medium, info Lowest; DOM: critical Highest, serious High, moderate Medium, minor Low; unknown Low | jira_export.py:32-47, 90, 104 |
| Workbook hotspot weights | Critical 4, Serious 3, Moderate 2, Minor 1 | xlsx_export.py:188 |
| UI impact chip colors (expert DOM views) | critical->critical token, serious->major, moderate->minor, minor->info; text shows raw axe word | routes/A11y.tsx:548-572 |
| CSV `severity` column | mixes scales: image critical/major/minor/info, DOM critical/serious/moderate/minor | csv_export.py:30 |
| Markdown inventory severity table | image scale only: critical, major, minor, info | markdown_report.py:84-88 |

Inconsistencies (severity):
- issues.py:104-105 comment says image "critical/major/minor/info -> critical/serious/moderate/minor", but the dict key "minor" is shared with axe, so an image `minor` weighs 1.0 (minor), not 2.0 (moderate). Exports map image minor to "Minor" (audit_report.py:95), Jira maps it to "Medium" (jira_export.py:35).
- Alfa rows (impact None) weigh 1.0 for priority (issues.py:1198) but are labeled severity "Moderate" in the audit report and workbook (audit_report.py:1364; xlsx_export.py:332).
- The Issues table has no severity column; impact is visible only indirectly through the Priority tier and in the expert DOM views (Issues.tsx:278-288).

##### B.2 Priority score and UI tiers

- Formula: `priority = round(weight(impact) * ln(1 + page_count), 3)` (issues.py:1188-1199). Occurrence count is not in the formula. Image rows use the number of distinct pages with an occurrence (issues.py:1038-1043, 1071).
- UI tier (Issues table "Priority" column and issue fact strip): High if >= 6, Medium if >= 3, else Low (Issues.tsx:818-823; IssueEvidence.tsx:294-300). The number itself is not shown (Issues.tsx:636-639).
- Hint text on the detail fact: "Severity × how many pages it touches. Fix sooner when both are high." (IssueEvidence.tsx:275).
- Informational rows show "n/a" (Issues.tsx:633-634; IssueEvidence.tsx:271-276).

Page-count thresholds derived from the formula (arithmetic computed with python3 from the code's formula; VERIFIED as arithmetic, not observed in UI):

| Impact weight | 1-page score / tier | Medium from | High from |
|---|---|---|---|
| critical (4) | 2.773 / Low | 2 pages | 4 pages |
| serious or image major (3) | 2.079 / Low | 2 pages | 7 pages |
| moderate (2) | 1.386 / Low | 4 pages | 20 pages |
| minor, image minor/info, Alfa None (1) | 0.693 / Low | 20 pages | 403 pages |

Consequence: any single-page issue shows Priority "Low", even a critical one.

Related inconsistencies:
- Workbook guidance claims "the table is sorted by Axcess's priority score (occurrence count, impact, and reach)" and to "fix issues in conformance-level order" within Likely Barrier (xlsx_export.py:129-137). Actual order is lane then priority (issues.py:1240), and occurrence count is not an input.
- Audit report cards are ordered by severity then affected pages, not the priority score (audit_report.py:388-389).

Difficulty: YAML effort `under_15m`->Beginner, `under_2h`->Intermediate, `multi_sprint`->Advanced, else "Unknown" (issues.py:112-116, 973, 1068). UI shows "n/a" for Unknown (Issues.tsx:659-663). Audit report effort words "Under 15 minutes" / "Under 2 hours" / "Multi-sprint" / fallback "Effort: see fix steps" (audit_report.py:1735-1743).

Responsibility (owner): YAML `owner` or default "dev" for DOM rows, "editor" for image rows (issues.py:971, 1066). Workbook display Developer/Editor/Designer/Content author (xlsx_export.py:118-123); audit worklist headings Developers/Content editors/Designers/Content team (audit_report.py:74-79).

##### B.3 Conformance level (A / AA / AAA / BP)

- Labels: A, AA, AAA, BP (best practice) (issues.py:47-52, 1179-1182; types.ts:680).
- Source order: YAML card `wcag_level` first, else stored finding `wcag_level` (issues.py:744-746). axe stores the strictest level tag the rule carries (AAA > AA > A) (axe.py:142-146); axe best-practice rules get level None -> BP (axe.py:95-104). Image rows take level from the `image_findings` YAML card only when classified and not adequate (issues.py:1027-1031). Legacy rows forced BP (issues.py:751, 756).
- UI: badge text A/AA/AAA/BP, title "WCAG conformance level" (components/ConformanceBadge.tsx:13-28). Issues "WCAG" column shows SC number plus badge, or "Best practice" when no SC (Issues.tsx:621-630). Level filter options "All (n)", "Level A (n)", "Level AA (n)", "Level AAA (n)", "Best practice (n)" (Issues.tsx:238-249). Detail fact "Criterion level" shows conformance, or "n/a" when no SC (IssueEvidence.tsx:269).
- Workbook "Conformance Level" column shows A/AA/AAA, and "S" for BP (xlsx_export.py:99-101, 529).
- Scan target shown as "Audited against: WCAG 2.2 Level {A|AA|AAA}" (audit_report.py:514; xlsx_export.py:940) and "Compliance Standard: WCAG 2.2 Level X" (xlsx_export.py:502). Default "AA" for old scans (exports/collector.py:138-140).
- API facet `conformance_counts` (issues.py:651-656; server.py:1838).

##### B.4 Finding status values

| Value | Workbook display | Meaning in code | Cite |
|---|---|---|---|
| new | Not Started | open, unreviewed | xlsx_export.py:103; audit_report.py:84, 86 |
| reviewing | Reviewing | open, unreviewed | same |
| in_progress | In Progress | expert confirmed an open barrier | audit_report.py:363-366; export_readiness.py:92-95 |
| remediated | Resolved | triaged | xlsx_export.py:106; audit_report.py:83 |
| accepted_risk | Accepted Risk | triaged | xlsx_export.py:107 |
| false_positive | Not an Issue | triaged, rejected | xlsx_export.py:108 |

- DB CHECK constraint on both finding tables (src/audit/db/migrations/0001_initial_schema.sql:81; 0002_page_a11y_findings.sql:42); server validation (src/audit/db/repo.py:352-361, 377-378).
- A rationale is required for in_progress, remediated, accepted_risk, false_positive; max 2000 chars; secrets redacted; "API responses never return the stored note" (repo.py:362, 365-393; frontend statusDecision.ts:3-8, 17-33 prompt "Document why ... should be marked ...").
- UI display: raw value with underscores replaced by spaces, neutral chip (components/ui.tsx:13-21; A11yByRule.tsx:146, 411; IssuePagesTable.tsx:375).
- Where a user can set status: expert views only. DOM finding select in the WCAG DOM-engine drill-down (routes/A11y.tsx:500-530), bulk per rule (A11yByRule.tsx:35-42, 395-411), image bulk per group (GroupedFindings.tsx:291-292), single image finding (FindingDetail.tsx:49-80, 253). API: POST /api/findings/{id}/status, /api/findings/bulk-status, /api/a11y-findings/bulk-status, /api/a11y-findings/{id}/status (server.py:1993, 2019, 2063, 2107). The Issues table and issue detail have no status control (no status mutation in Issues.tsx or IssueEvidence.tsx; they only display counts in the pages table, IssuePagesTable.tsx:358-378).
- The issues API accepts `status=` (issue included if any finding has it) but the Issues UI does not expose it (server.py:1804; issues.py:330-331; Issues.tsx:52-56).

#### C. Exports

##### C.1 Formats and how to get them

| Format key | UI (Export button menu on Overview and Issues) | API | CLI `audit export -f` | Filename | Cite |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| xlsx | "Remediation workbook", hint "Excel · one row per issue, with fixes" | GET /api/scans/{id}/export/xlsx | yes | scan_{id}.xlsx | ExportMenu.tsx:21; server.py:416-445; cli.py:670-676 |
| audit | "Audit report", hint "Markdown · narrative report" | /export/audit | NO (not in `_EXPORT_FORMATS`) | scan_{id}.audit.md | ExportMenu.tsx:22; server.py:425, 443; cli.py:670 |
| csv | "Issue table", hint "CSV · one row per occurrence" | /export/csv | yes (CLI default) | scan_{id}.csv | ExportMenu.tsx:23; cli.py:689 |
| json | "Raw findings", hint "JSON · full evidence payload" | /export/json | yes | scan_{id}.json | ExportMenu.tsx:24 |
| jira | not in UI | /export/jira | yes | scan_{id}.jira.csv | server.py:419, 441 |
| markdown (evidence inventory) | not in UI | /export/markdown | yes | scan_{id}.md | server.py:420, 442 |
| webhook | no UI, API, or CLI trigger | none | none | n/a | see below |
| protected redacted summary | protected report page: card "Authorized redacted export", button "Download redacted summary" | POST /api/protected-scans/{id}/exports/redacted | refused | protected_scan_{id}_redacted.md | ProtectedCompanion.tsx:833, 860; protected_api.py:546, 595; cli.py:723-734 |

- Export menu button text "Export" (ExportMenu.tsx:76); it is placed on Overview (ScanDetail.tsx:231) and Issues (Issues.tsx:136).
- Protected scans: ordinary export route returns 403 "Protected reports require an authorized redacted export workflow." (server.py:2339-2347). Redacted summary sections: "# Axcess protected report, redacted operational summary", "## Export boundary", "## Report metadata", "## Coverage summary", "## Manually documented WCAG check outcomes", "## Redacted issue index" (columns Source layer, Rule, WCAG SC, Level, Outcome, Impact, Occurrences, Indexed pages), "## Verification and sharing" (src/audit/protected/export.py:107, 115, 159, 187). UI states it "omits target URLs, page locations, selectors, screenshots, OCR text, and all browser or session material" (ProtectedCompanion.tsx:835).
- Webhook: `AUDIT_WEBHOOK_URL` / optional `AUDIT_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`, body = JSON export payload plus `source` (exports/webhook.py:26-39, 42-87). No call site anywhere in src/ (grep for `webhook` outside the module finds only the package docstring and a json_export comment). So the webhook is not wired to any scan completion path. VERIFIED by grep; docs/developer-guide.md:52 calls it "env-gated, opt-in".

Draft gating (API only):
- A final export requires the expert evaluation `completed` and no blockers (incl. A.2 unreviewed-evidence rule). Otherwise 409 unless `?draft=acknowledged` (export_readiness.py:57-80; server.py:2352-2359).
- The UI menu always sends `draft=acknowledged` (ExportMenu.tsx:88-92), so UI downloads never 409; they are labeled drafts instead.
- Draft labeling: filename `scan_{id}_DRAFT.{ext}` (export_readiness.py:141-144); header `X-Axcess-Export-State: draft` (server.py:2381-2382); Markdown/audit get a blockquote "**DRAFT, INCOMPLETE ACCESSIBILITY EVALUATION**" (export_readiness.py:24-27, 164-171); JSON gets `export_notice` {label, draft, evaluation_status, warning} (172-187); xlsx gets a first sheet "DRAFT NOTICE" (188, 214-243); CSV and Jira get an extra last column "Axcess export state" = DRAFT (192, 199-211). Golden diffs: tests/unit/golden/rich_scan_DRAFT.*.diff.
- CLI `export` performs no readiness check and no draft labeling (cli.py:680-761 has no export_readiness import or call). VERIFIED by reading.

##### C.2 Structure per format

CSV (src/audit/exports/csv_export.py:27-59), one header row, 24 columns in this order:
`finding_kind, finding_id, severity, status, wcag_criterion, wcag_level, page_url, ui_url, priority_score, classification, alt_adequacy, alt_text, above_fold, image_url, content_hash, ocr_text, ocr_confidence, vlm_rationale, remediation_hint, rule_id, target_selector, failure_summary, help_url, revealed_by`.
- Image rows: `finding_kind=image_of_text`, one row per (finding, page occurrence) (csv_export.py:69-81, 102). DOM rows: `finding_kind=wcag_<pipeline>` (e.g. wcag_axe, wcag_alfa, wcag_semantic, wcag_keyboard), one row per stored finding (csv_export.py:83-84, 133). Golden rich_scan.csv has image_of_text 6, wcag_axe 74, wcag_semantic 3, wcag_alfa 2, wcag_keyboard 1 rows (counted with `cut | uniq -c`).
- No lane, confidence, issue group, or "What to fix" column. The menu label "Issue table" is per finding, not per issue group.

JSON (json_export.py:20, 37-63): `schema_version` 4 (v4 added `revealed_by`, json_export.py:16-20), `scan` {id, seed_url, status, started_at, finished_at, page_count, finding_count, error_count, by_severity, axe_pages_scanned, axe_violations_total, alfa_pages_scanned, alfa_failed_total, alfa_cant_tell_total, by_wcag_level}, `findings` (image, with `occurrences`), `a11y_findings` (fields per collector.py:67-106: id, scan_id, rule_id, wcag_sc, wcag_scs, wcag_level, impact, help, help_url, target_selector, failure_summary, html_snippet, status, page_id, page_url, page_title, ui_url, pipeline, engine_outcome, engine_evidence_json, engine_evidence_status, target_display, manual_review_hint, revealed_by). Keys sorted. No lane field.

Jira CSV (jira_export.py): columns Summary, Description, Priority, Issue Type, Labels, Component (21-28). Issue Type "Bug", Component "Accessibility" (50-51). One row per finding; skips remediated/accepted_risk/false_positive (67, 86-87, 99-100). DOM Summary "WCAG {rule_id}, SC {sc}: {page_url}" or ", best-practice" (111-113). DOM Description fields in order: Source, Outcome, Rule, Impact, WCAG SC, Description, Page, To reproduce, Target selector, Why it failed (Diagnostic for Alfa), Failing HTML, Rule docs, "Review locally: {url}" (116-158). Image Summary "WCAG 1.4.5: image of text, {OCR}" and variants (180-190); image Description: Severity (priority score), Criterion, Classification, Alt adequacy, Image URL, OCR text, VLM rationale, Suggested fix, Occurrences, Review locally (193-224). Labels (161-176, 227-233).

Markdown evidence inventory (markdown_report.py): "# Accessibility evidence inventory, Scan #N" (41); disclaimer "This is a raw, status-bearing evidence inventory, including review leads and informational records. Use the stakeholder audit export for the expert-reviewed remediation worklist; neither artifact certifies conformance." (45-49); "## Scan metadata" (51); "## Executive summary" + Severity table critical/major/minor/info (81-88); "## Top N image-analysis records" (TOP_N 20) (21, 91); "## All image-analysis evidence" table `# | Status | Severity | Score | Classification | Adequacy | Image` (100-106); "## WCAG DOM-engine findings" with WCAG level table A/AA/AAA/best-practice and scope reminder (125-146); "### Top N DOM-engine findings" (TOP_A11Y_N 30) blocks with Source, Outcome, Rule, Page, Target, Why it failed/Diagnostic, Evidence, Docs, Status, Review (22, 150, 197-221); "### All DOM-engine findings" table `# | Source | Outcome | Impact | WCAG SC | Level | Rule | Page` (156-158). No revealed_by.

Audit report (audit_report.py:489-567; golden tests/unit/golden/rich_scan.audit.md): title "# Accessibility audit, Scan #N"; Generated line; Seed URL; "Audited against"; "Pages crawled"; "Detection methods used" (pipeline labels at audit_report.py:112-121); optional blocked-seed warning; sections in order: "## Executive summary" (699), "## Open barrier summary" (Level table `Level | Open issue types | What it means` and POUR table) (765-821), "## Who is affected" (826), "## Coverage and method" with "### States behind a click" (868, 949), "## WCAG 2.2 A/AA coverage, what's automated vs. manual" with "### Automated & AI-assisted (N criteria)" and "### Needs manual testing (N criteria)" (1014-1044), optional "## Expert evaluation record" (579-640), "## Page hotspots" (1078), "## Remediation worklist by owner" (1136), "## Issue cards" (541), "## Appendix A, Findings dropped during self-critique" table `Method | Issue | WCAG | Reason set aside` (1274-1306), "## Appendix B, Review leads and informational evidence" (1310), closing "**Scope note.** ... a clean run is **necessary, not sufficient** for conformance." (561-564).
Issue card fields (audit_report.py:1173-1265): "### {n}. {title}", optional "> ⚠ **Human review needed**, ..." (Alfa, or no templated fix) (1177-1191), "**WCAG:**", "**Detected by:**", "**Where:** N finding(s) on **P** page(s).", "Specific locations:" (each "- **Page:** ... **Location on page:** ... **Technical target:** ... **Seen after:** ... **Observed evidence:** ...", 1561-1583), "**What is happening:**", "**Why it matters:**", "**Affects:**", "**Severity:** {word}, {reason}", "**Effort:**", "**Owner:**", "**Fix (do this):**" numbered steps, "**Verify it is fixed:**" (Manual / Automated / Acceptance), "**My confidence:** {High|Medium|Low}." (from YAML `confidence_default`, default medium, 1398), "_Rule docs: {url}_". Max 10 locations per card (65).

Workbook (xlsx) sheet order (xlsx_export.py:1393-1457; golden sheet_names in tests/unit/golden/rich_scan.xlsx.json): [DRAFT NOTICE if draft], Summary, Issues Overview, one tab per issue named "I01 {title...}" (max 40 tabs, xlsx_export.py:171), More Issues (only if > 40 issues), Page Hotspots, Page References, DOM States, Who's Affected, Coverage & Method, Test Tracking, Manual Review Evidence.

| Sheet | Columns / fields (exact) | Cite |
|---|---|---|
| Summary | sections "Scan" (Seed URL, Audited against, Pages crawled, Audit date), optional "Expert evaluation" (Target, Review status, Reviewer, Purpose, Included scope, Excluded scope, Sample, Methods, Limitations), "Issue counts" (Likely-barrier issue groups, Likely-barrier occurrences, Review-only / informational groups, Review-only / informational occurrences, Already-triaged issue types), "Likely barriers by severity" (Critical, Serious, Moderate, Minor), "Likely barriers by WCAG level", "Likely barriers by WCAG principle (POUR)", "WCAG 2.2 A/AA coverage (all 55 criteria)" (Automated, Partly automated, AI-assisted, Manual only) | xlsx_export.py:936-1000; golden Summary A1-B48 |
| Issues Overview | meta rows "Page", "Compliance Standard", "Audit Date", "Auditor" (default "Axcess"), "Prioritization Guidance"; header row 8: ID, Issue, Severity, Conformance Level, Remediation Ownership, Status, Instances, Pages, Details. Status cell has a dropdown (Not Started, Reviewing, In Progress, Resolved, Accepted Risk, Not an Issue); Status value = the most common status in the group; Details link "Open instances" or "See More Issues" | xlsx_export.py:141-151, 486-560 (meta 500-506, status dropdown 556-560), 110-117, 208-213, 536 |
| Per-issue tab | title "Ixx · {title}", link "← Back to Issues Overview", block: Current Behavior, Severity, Category (POUR principle), Impact, Should be (acceptance criteria) (only if authored), Instances, Pages, Environment, References, Rule documentation; table "Instances, one row per occurrence found": #, Where, User action, Element, What to fix, How to reproduce; optional "Suggested fix" table: Option, Applies to, Approach, Watch out for (only when YAML `fix_options` exist); first circled screenshot embedded when available | xlsx_export.py:155-162, 623-737 (block 658-675, table 693-720), 563-620 |
| More Issues | ID, Issue, #, Where, User action, Element, What to fix | xlsx_export.py:173, 740-757 |
| Page Hotspots | Page, Title, Weighted load, Findings shown | xlsx_export.py:1035 |
| Page References | Issue, Detection source, Page, Page title, Location on page, Technical target, Occurrences, Status (every issue in every lane) | xlsx_export.py:1128, 1199-1208 |
| DOM States | Page, Controls found, Controls operated, States, Why it stopped | xlsx_export.py:1070, 1101; golden title "DOM states, content behind a click" |
| Who's Affected | User group, Issue types affecting them, Across (page-instances) | xlsx_export.py:1243 |
| Coverage & Method | SC, Criterion, Level, Coverage, What Axcess does, Still verify by hand | xlsx_export.py:1277-1282 |
| Test Tracking | Focus, What to check, Pass / Fail (choices Pass, Fail, Needs follow-up, N/A, Not tested) | xlsx_export.py:176-178 |
| Manual Review Evidence | SC, Criterion, Outcome, Rationale, Page reference, External reference, Expert note | xlsx_export.py:1333-1339 |

##### C.3 "What to fix" and "User impact"

- "What to fix" exists ONLY in the workbook: column 5 of the per-issue "Instances" table (xlsx_export.py:160) and column 7 of "More Issues" (xlsx_export.py:173). Its value is the issue's authored fix steps as "• " bullets, identical on every instance row, blank when the rule has no authored steps (xlsx_export.py:216-220, 475, 482; golden rich_scan.xlsx.json: I01 cell E16 is None, I04 E17 starts "• Open the affected page in DevTools..."). It is not per-instance guidance.
- Same content is labeled differently elsewhere: "Expected behavior" in the UI (IssueEvidence.tsx:173; Issues.tsx:738), "**Fix (do this):**" in the audit report (audit_report.py:1243).
- "How to reproduce" (xlsx_export.py:161) is filled from `verify_manual` or `verify_automated`, i.e. verification steps, not reproduction (xlsx_export.py:354-358). Reproduction of click-revealed findings is in "User action".
- "User impact": no column or field with this name exists in src/, frontend, YAML, or goldens (repo-wide grep, case-insensitive). The phrase occurs only in audit report prose: "prioritize user impact and foundational dependencies" and "triage promptly alongside actual user impact" (audit_report.py:787, 794; golden scan_audit.md:24, 28). The nearest field is the workbook issue-tab row "Impact" (xlsx_export.py:661), filled from `why_matters`, fallback "Users relying on assistive technology hit a barrier here." (xlsx_export.py:340-342).
- Stale doc: docs/user-guide.md:113-116 says Issues Overview has "conformance level, owner, status, user impact, locations, action, resources" columns; code shows ID, Issue, Severity, Conformance Level, Remediation Ownership, Status, Instances, Pages, Details. docs/user-guide.md:102-110 says the scan detail page offers CSV, JSON, Jira CSV, Markdown; the UI menu offers only workbook, audit report, CSV, JSON (ExportMenu.tsx:20-25).

##### C.4 What the goldens show

- tests/unit/golden/: final exports `scan.*` and `rich_scan.*` (csv, jira.csv, json, md, xlsx.json fingerprint; audit.md) and draft variants stored only as unified diffs `*_DRAFT.*.diff` (tests/unit/test_export_goldens.py:16-24, 223-230). The xlsx golden is a JSON fingerprint of cells/styles/sheet names, not a binary.
- rich_scan.xlsx.json: 49 sheets final (Summary, Issues Overview, I01..I40, More Issues, 7 rollups); draft adds "DRAFT NOTICE" first.
- rich_scan.audit.md: section order as in C.2; Appendix B lines show raw lane labels (1866-1874).
- tests/ui/golden/api_surface.json and api_openapi.json pin route `/api/scans/{scan_id:int}/export/{fmt}`; api_contract.json pins `review_lane` and `review_lane_counts` fields.
- UNVERIFIED: whether goldens currently pass. Reason: tests were not run (read-only brief).

#### D. "Revealed by" (control that had to be operated)

##### D.1 Data

- Column `page_a11y_findings.revealed_by`: NULL = visible at page load; non-NULL = "the accessible name of the control an auditor must operate to reproduce it" (src/audit/db/migrations/0022_interaction_revealed_by.sql:14-19).
- First writer wins; the upsert never overwrites it, and the load-state pass runs first (repo.py:592-598; migration 0022:16-18).
- Value = control label computed in the page: aria-labelledby text, else aria-label, else associated <label> text, else textContent, else value, else title; whitespace collapsed; cut to 300 chars (src/audit/analyzer/interaction/probe.py:162-166, 183). Unnamed control falls back to "<tag>", e.g. "<button>" (probe.py:758; interaction/base.py:43-48). Configured search pass writes "Configured search" (crawler/search.py:155).
- Only DOM (axe path) findings carry it; image findings never do (csv_export.py:117-124).
- Captured DOM states: table `page_dom_states` with `revealed_by` and `path_labels` (ordered chain of controls) (migrations/0028_page_dom_states.sql:21-31; web/page_inspector.py:110-136).
- Issue rows expose it per location sample (first 3 unique locations per issue) as `IssueLocation.revealed_by` (issues.py:176-183, 1098-1134).

##### D.2 What the user sees, where

| Surface | Exact wording | Cite |
|---|---|---|
| Issue evidence "Flagged element" card (issue detail page and the Issues list expansion) | under the page name: After clicking “{label}” (only for up to 3 sample locations) | components/IssueEvidence.tsx:211-231 |
| Page evidence route (/scans/:id/pages/:pageId) | findings grouped under headings "At page load (N findings)" then "After clicking “{label}” (N findings)", in probe order; grouping appears only if something was revealed | routes/PageEvidence.tsx:73-75, 139-146, 364-386 |
| Page inspector state picker | select labeled "Page state": "At page load (N)" and "After clicking “A” → “B” (N)" (full control chain); note "Captured during the scan, after the control was operated." | routes/Inspector.tsx:563-603 |
| Page inspector, element not found (all targets revealed) | "This element was first flagged after activating “{label}”. This capture is the page as it loaded, so it may not appear here." (plural form for several) | Inspector.tsx:335-347; tests/ui/test_inspector_revealed_state.py:81-98 |
| Page inspector, mixed | "Some of these were first flagged after a control was operated, so they may not be in this capture of the page as it loaded; the rest may have changed since the scan." | Inspector.tsx:322-333; test file:117-137 |
| Page inspector, other state count | "{n} more occurrence(s) of this issue is/are in another state." | Inspector.tsx:736-743; test file:197-223 |
| Page inspector, state not captured | "This interaction state was not captured" + "To reach it by hand, open the live page and follow the steps in the state list above. Switch back to At page load for the markup this report does hold." | Inspector.tsx:532-541 |
| Overview method ledger, row labeled "Click Through DOM States" (server.py:3706-3707) | "Found: {n} issue group(s) with evidence that only appears after a control is used." or "Found: no issue in this report depends on a state that only appears after a click." | components/MethodCoverageLedger.tsx:173-186 |
| Overview stat tile | "DOM States Found", hint "Reached by operating controls" | routes/ScanDetail.tsx:353-357 |
| Workbook per-issue tab and More Issues, column "User action" | Open "{label}" on this page. / Load the page. | xlsx_export.py:480; golden I04 C19 `Open "Show more" on this page.`, More Issues E12 `Open "Open menu" on this page.` |
| Workbook "DOM States" sheet | per-page Controls found / operated / States / Why it stopped | xlsx_export.py:1070, 1101 |
| Audit report location line | **Seen after:** activating "{label}" on this page. | audit_report.py:1576-1581 |
| Audit report coverage | "### States behind a click" with row "Findings visible only after a click" | audit_report.py:949, 964 |
| Jira description | **To reproduce:** Load the page, then activate "{label}". (or "Load the page.") | jira_export.py:139-142; exports/interaction_coverage.py:192-196 |
| CSV | last column `revealed_by` (empty when load state) | csv_export.py:53-58, 159 |
| JSON | `a11y_findings[].revealed_by` (null when load state) | collector.py:101-104; json_export.py:16-20 |
| Markdown evidence inventory | not shown | markdown_report.py (no reference) |

Wording inconsistency: the same fact is phrased "After clicking" (UI), "Open ... on this page." (workbook), "activating" (audit report, Jira, inspector). The inspector deliberately says "first flagged after", not "appears after" (Inspector.tsx:312-316), while the ledger says "evidence that only appears after a control is used" (MethodCoverageLedger.tsx:183).

##### E.2 Overview (completed report)

- Header: "Overview", meta "{site} · Completed {time}", actions "Export" menu and "Open Issue Groups" (ScanDetail.tsx:201-236).
- Tiles: "Pages Tested" (hint "{n} crawl errors"), "Issues Found" (= total occurrences across all lanes, `occurrence_counts.all_evidence`), "Issue Groups" (= number of issue rows, all lanes), "DOM States Found" (hint "Reached by operating controls") (ScanDetail.tsx:166-167, 336-358; server.py:1842-1843).
- Card "What this scan actually checked": "{x} of {y} methods ran. Open a row for what it does and does not prove." Each row expands to description, "Found: ..." line with "See them in Issues", and a caveat (MethodCoverageLedger.tsx:61-150).
- Disclosure "Expert tools and scan details": "DOM engines", "Image evidence (N)", "Observed reviewer rejection rate: ... This is a result from this report, not a general detector-accuracy claim.", nested "Danger zone" with "Delete report" (ScanDetail.tsx:367-417).

##### E.3 Issues page

- Header meta: "{site} · {n} issue groups (or 'x of n issue groups') · {m} occurrences | Evidence for expert review, not a conformance verdict." (Issues.tsx:116-134; ReportHeader.tsx:79-93).
- If Alfa rows exist: disclosure "What is an ACT rule?" explaining pass/fail/cannot tell (Issues.tsx:139-160).
- Toolbar: search input (label "Search issues", placeholder "Search issue name or WCAG criterion"; matches title or SC substring), select "Level", select "Type", button "Clear filters" (Issues.tsx:229-274, 871-886; issues.py:332-334).
- Columns, in order: Issue, Type, WCAG, Priority, Pages, Occurrences, Difficulty, Responsibility, About (Issues.tsx:278-288). All except About sortable by header button; default Priority desc, lane first (Issues.tsx:299-321, 405-419).
- Cell content: Issue = link to full evidence; Type = lane tag; WCAG = SC + badge or "Best practice"; Priority = High/Medium/Low (n/a informational); Pages = link "{n} page(s)" to the pages view; Occurrences = number; Difficulty = Beginner/Intermediate/Advanced or n/a; Responsibility = capitalized owner or n/a; About = expand button (Issues.tsx:589-686).
- "About" panel: "What it is", lane note, "Why it matters:", "Expected behavior" (numbered fix steps), "Done when:" (acceptance), "Abilities affected:", links "Full evidence record" and "Rule docs" (Issues.tsx:703-785).
- 10 rows per page with pagination (components/TablePagination.tsx:10; Issues.tsx:557).
- Empty states: "No issues match these filters. Clear a filter to see more results." / "No issue groups were detected. Check scan coverage before drawing a conformance conclusion." (Issues.tsx:188-192).
- API filters not in the UI: responsibility, abilities, status (server.py:1801-1804).

##### E.4 Issue detail (full evidence record)

Header: conformance badge (not for informational) + title; meta "WCAG SC {sc}: {name}" (IssueDetail.tsx:62-78). Body (components/IssueEvidence.tsx), top to bottom:
1. Lane card: badge + lane label, "{confidence} evidence confidence" chip, "Rule docs" link, evidence summary sentence, lane note (68-104).
2. Fact strip: Criterion level, Priority (hint "Severity × how many pages it touches. Fix sooner when both are high."), Pages affected, Occurrences, Difficulty (if known), Responsibility, Abilities affected (non-informational only for several) (110-131, 267-288).
3. "Pages with this issue" table: #, Page title, Page URL, Open live page, Stored evidence, Occurrences, Issue screenshots, Status (Status hidden for informational), with its own search and sort (133-151; IssuePagesTable.tsx:163-172).
4. "What it is" ("Evidence summary" for informational) plus collapsed disclosure "Why it matters, and how to fix it" (expert_review: "Why it matters, and what to check") containing why it matters, "Expected behavior" steps, "Done when:", and "How to verify" / "What to check to confirm" (manual and automated verification) (153-207; disclosure collapsed by default, components/ui.tsx:405, 425).
5. "Flagged element": up to 3 sample locations with page, "After clicking “...”" when revealed, selector, highlighted HTML snippet, context (211-248).
- Missing issue key: "Evidence group not found" with "Back to issue table" (IssueDetail.tsx:39-54).
- Alfa rows with no YAML get default verification text "Review the stored Alfa evidence and manually test the mapped WCAG success criterion." / "Run the same Alfa rule in a fresh scan and compare its source-attributed outcomes." (issues.py:433-449).
- Longform content (what/why/fix/acceptance/verify) comes from rules/audit_report.yaml: 12 axe rule cards, 7 image cards (essential_missing, essential_inadequate, essential_partial, essential_adequate, informational_missing, logo_missing, decorative_missing), 10 SC-keyed cards under semantic_criteria (2.1.2, 2.4.4, 1.4.10, 2.4.11, 2.4.3, 1.4.2, 2.2.2, 1.3.2, 1.4.4, 1.4.12) (src/audit/rules/audit_report.yaml:34, 703, 917-1316; counted with awk). Rules without a card render with empty longform and title fallback (issues.py:1243-1249).

## Appendix C: Rule engines and browser probes

Source file: agent notes `checks_rules_visual.md`, copied verbatim with headings demoted.

### Axcess checks: rule engines and browser probes (verified facts)

Repo: `/home/user/axcess`. All paths below are relative to the repo root. Line
numbers were read on 2026-09-24 from the working tree (clean `git status`).

#### How each fact was verified

- **VERIFIED-RUN**: I executed something and saw the output. The only things
  executed were (a) Python `yaml` parses of the rules files and (b) the
  vendored `src/audit/web/static/axe.min.js` loaded in Node 22.22.2 (no browser,
  no DOM, no network), calling axe's own `axe.utils.ruleShouldRun` with the exact
  tag lists that `src/audit/analyzer/axe.py` builds. Scripts live in the
  scratchpad (`axe_rules_check.cjs`, `axe_ts.cjs`, `axe_aaa.cjs`).
- **VERIFIED-READ**: I read the source or test and the fact follows directly.
- **UNVERIFIED**: I could not confirm it; the reason is given.

Environment limits: there is no `.venv`, no Playwright Chromium
(`~/.cache/ms-playwright` absent) and no `src/audit/alfa_runner/node_modules`.
So **no pytest suite was run**, no page was rendered, and the Alfa rule
catalogue could not be listed. Test citations below show what the tests assert,
not that they pass today.

#### Main table

Lanes come from `src/audit/web/issues.py`: default `expert_review` (line 764);
axe rows set `likely_barrier` (946-952); Alfa `failed` sets `likely_barrier`
(896-901) while Alfa `cant_tell` keeps the default; the `responsive` (829-832),
`focus` (833-838) and `visual` (839-880) branches never override the default,
except legacy visual motion rows without a runtime measurement, which become
`informational` (718-725, 840-846).

#### Rule engines: details

##### axe-core

- **Version**: `axe v4.10.2`, from the bundle header (`src/audit/web/static/axe.min.js:1`),
  confirmed as `axe.version` = 4.10.2 when loaded in Node (VERIFIED-RUN). The file
  is committed to git (`git ls-files` lists it). Note: `axe.py:235` says to run
  `make setup` to fetch it, but the `setup` target (`Makefile:19-23`) does not
  fetch axe. The frontend dev lockfile resolves a different axe-core 4.11.3
  (`src/audit/web/frontend/package-lock.json:2154`); that one is for UI tests, not
  scans.
- **On by default**: `CrawlConfig.axe_enabled = True`, `axe_level = "AA"`
  (`orchestrator.py:208-209`). CLI `--skip-axe` default False, `--axe-level`
  default AA (`src/audit/cli.py:302-318`, wired at `:518-519`). Web form:
  `scan_engine` defaults to `axe` (`src/audit/web/server.py:2877-2880`,
  `:2908-2909`; React default `src/audit/web/frontend/src/components/newScan/scanPolicy.ts:80`, `:88`).
  Protected login scans: `scan_engine` default `axe`, level AA
  (`server.py:149-150`, `:1219-1220`). A missing bundle disables axe with a note
  rather than failing (`orchestrator.py:467-480`).
- **Tag pack** (`axe.py:72-104`): A = `wcag2a, wcag21a, wcag22a`; AA adds
  `wcag2aa, wcag21aa, wcag22aa`; AAA adds `wcag2aaa`. `best-practice` is always
  added. Passed as `runOnly: {type: 'tag'}` (`axe.py:291-300`).
- **Rules run** (VERIFIED-RUN, using axe's own selection function): 104 rules in
  the bundle; A = 84, AA = 90, AAA = 93 (each count includes 28 best-practice
  rules). AA adds over A: `autocomplete-valid, avoid-inline-spacing,
  color-contrast, meta-viewport, target-size, valid-lang`. AAA adds
  `color-contrast-enhanced, identical-links-same-purpose, meta-refresh-no-exceptions`.
- **Rules not run at AA** (14, VERIFIED-RUN): axe's default `tagExclude` is
  `["experimental","deprecated"]`, so these never run unless those tags are
  requested, which Axcess never does:
  - experimental: `css-orientation-lock` (1.3.4), `label-content-name-mismatch`
    (2.5.3), `p-as-heading`, `table-fake-caption`, `td-has-header` (1.3.1),
    `focus-order-semantics`, `hidden-content` (best-practice)
  - deprecated: `aria-roledescription`, `audio-caption`, `duplicate-id`,
    `duplicate-id-active`
  - AAA only: the three AAA rules listed above
- **Explicit disables or filters in Axcess**: none. No `rules: {...}` overrides and
  no `axe.configure` in `src/audit` (grep, VERIFIED-READ). Only `violations` are
  kept (`axe.py:288-298`); axe `incomplete` (needs-review) results are discarded.
  The Alfa browser fixture shows axe returning `incomplete` for contrast over a
  sized gradient (`tests/integration/test_alfa_browser.py:54`), which a real scan
  would not store.
- **target-size runs**: yes at AA and AAA, no at A (VERIFIED-RUN). It is
  `enabled: false` in the axe bundle with tags `cat.sensory-and-visual-cues,
  wcag22aa, wcag258`, but axe's tag matcher selects any rule that matches an
  included tag regardless of its `enabled` flag. It is the only rule in axe
  4.10.2 tagged `wcag22a` or `wcag22aa` (VERIFIED-RUN). Not confirmed in a live
  browser (no Chromium here).
- **Storage**: `page_a11y_findings` via `repo.upsert_axe_violation`
  (`src/audit/db/repo.py:543-625`), which does not write `pipeline`, so the column
  default `'axe'` applies (`src/audit/db/migrations/0003_pipeline_discriminator.sql:21`).
  Upsert key is `(page_id, rule_id, target_hash)`. One row per rule and DOM node
  (`axe.py:311-342`); HTML snippet capped at 4000 characters (`axe.py:330`).
  Primary SC is the lowest numbered SC tag; stored level is the strictest level
  tag (`axe.py:107-147`).
- **Re-runs**: the interaction probe re-runs axe after clicking controls; those
  rows are ordinary `axe` rows with `revealed_by` set (`orchestrator.py:263-280`,
  `:1404-1414`). Search journeys also call axe (`src/audit/crawler/search.py:152`).
- **Static-only mode** skips axe and the probes on statically fetched pages
  (`orchestrator.py:203-206`; `cli.py:525-531`).

##### Siteimprove Alfa

- **Optional, off by default**: `alfa_enabled: bool = False`
  (`orchestrator.py:210-213`). Enabled only by choosing engine `alfa` or `both`
  in the web form (`server.py:2877-2880`, `:2910`) or protected scan request
  (`server.py:149`, `:1221`). The CLI has no Alfa option (`grep -i alfa
  src/audit/cli.py` returns nothing). Choosing `alfa` alone also turns off the
  interaction probe (`server.py:2911-2918`; `ChecksGroup.tsx:53`).
- **Install**: `make alfa-install` runs `npm ci` in `src/audit/alfa_runner`
  (`Makefile:83-84`). Needs Node 22 or later (`src/audit/alfa_runner/package.json:7-9`;
  `alfa.py:110-111`). Nothing is downloaded at scan time (`alfa.py:9-12`). If
  selected but unavailable, the crawl raises before starting
  (`orchestrator.py:482-486`); a per-page failure adds a scan note instead
  (`orchestrator.py:1353-1359`).
- **Pinned versions** (`package.json:10-18`, matched in `package-lock.json:23-25`,
  `:836-838`, `:969-971`, `:1341-1343`, `:1409-1411`): `@siteimprove/alfa-act`,
  `alfa-json`, `alfa-refinement`, `alfa-rules`, `alfa-wcag` all 0.118.0;
  `@siteimprove/alfa-playwright` 0.84.1; `playwright` 1.61.1.
- **Rule set**: every rule in `@siteimprove/alfa-rules` whose requirements include
  a WCAG criterion matching `Conformance.isA()`, `isAA()` (default) or `isAAA()`
  (`runner.mjs:168-176`). The level passed is the scan's `axe_level`
  (`orchestrator.py:1339`). No rules are disabled by Axcess (the one-rule slice at
  `runner.mjs:177-179` only applies to a desktop build diagnostic mode).
- **Outcomes kept**: only `failed` and `cantTell`; `passed` and `inapplicable` are
  counted but not stored (`evidence.mjs:90-101`). Max 200 findings per page,
  failures first (`evidence.mjs:8`, `:99`; `alfa.py:371-383`). Evidence JSON
  bounded to 4000 bytes in the runner and 12000 bytes in Python
  (`evidence.mjs:9`, `:25-52`; `alfa_evidence.py:85-110`).
- **Capture**: separate headless Chromium, viewport 1440 x 900, waits for `load`
  (30 s) then up to 10 s for network idle; non-2xx or non-HTML pages return no
  findings (`runner.mjs:85-95`, `:134-161`). Timeout 75 s, concurrency 1
  (`orchestrator.py:214-215`; `alfa.py:125-136`).
- **Storage**: `page_a11y_findings` with `pipeline = 'alfa'`, `impact` NULL,
  `engine_outcome` (`failed` or `cant_tell`), `engine_evidence_json`, `help_url` =
  rule URI (`repo.py:1038-1100`; `orchestrator.py:1922-1952`).
- **Issue grouping**: grouped by `(pipeline, rule_id, outcome)`, key
  `alfa:<rule>:<failed|cant_tell>` (`src/audit/web/a11y_queries.py:362-369`;
  `issues.py:881-883`). Alfa rows never borrow YAML remediation cards
  (`issues.py:729-732`).
- **Known limitation** (docs): Alfa returned 40 `cantTell` contrast results and
  zero failures on one report because of `background-size`; this is inability to
  calculate, not a pass or fail (`docs/alfa-evidence-review.md:7-25`).
- **UNVERIFIED**: the number and names of Alfa rules selected at A, AA, AAA, and
  their SC mappings, because `node_modules` is not installed. Only `sia-r69` and
  `sia-r12` are named in fixtures (`fixture.mjs:28`; `test_alfa_browser.py:48`);
  `sia-r11` appears only as seeded test data (`test_issues_view.py:604-612`).
  Also UNVERIFIED whether `Conformance.isAA()` includes criteria new in WCAG 2.2.

#### Coverage matrix (`src/audit/rules/wcag_coverage.yaml`, `src/audit/coverage_matrix.py`)

- Scope: WCAG 2.2, levels A and AA only; 4.1.1 excluded; AAA out of scope
  (`wcag_coverage.yaml:28-34`). Validated to 55 criteria, 31 A and 24 AA
  (`tests/unit/test_coverage_matrix.py:21-46`).
- Method values are exactly `automated`, `partial`, `ai-assisted`, `manual`
  (`coverage_matrix.py:28`). There is no plain "assisted" value.
- Counts (VERIFIED-RUN, yaml parse): 55 total; automated 5, partial 18,
  ai-assisted 6, manual 26. Level A: automated 2, partial 11, ai-assisted 4,
  manual 14. Level AA: automated 3, partial 7, ai-assisted 2, manual 12.
- Allowed pipelines: `axe, keyboard, responsive, focus, visual, image, semantic`
  (`coverage_matrix.py:29-37`). **`alfa` is not an allowed pipeline**, so the
  matrix attributes no criterion to Alfa. Alfa is also absent from the shipped
  pipeline list at `src/audit/web/coverage_status.py:54-121`.

Criteria attributed to a check in this scope (line = the `- sc:` line). "axe
rules" is the VERIFIED-RUN list of runnable axe 4.10.2 rules tagged with that SC.

#### Remediation text

- `src/audit/rules/remediation.yaml` holds **no rule ids**. It is keyed only on
  image-of-text `(classification, adequacy)` pairs, with a `"*"` fallback
  (`remediation.yaml:1-5`, entries `:7-153`), loaded by
  `src/audit/synthesizer/rules.py:1-18`. No check in this scope has text there.
- Per-rule remediation cards live in `src/audit/rules/audit_report.yaml`, loaded
  by `issues.py:118`:
  - `axe_rules` (12 keys, `:34-702`): `image-alt` (36), `color-contrast` (93),
    `link-name` (151), `label` (211), `html-has-lang` (272), `tabindex` (322),
    `target-size` (372), `link-in-text-block` (423), `region` (477),
    `landmark-one-main` (527), `page-has-heading-one` (571), `heading-order` (641).
  - `semantic_criteria` keyed by SC, used as the fallback for probe rules through
    `issues.py:733-738`: `1.4.10` (1018), `1.4.4` (1242), `1.4.12` (1280) for
    responsive; `2.4.11` (1062), `2.4.3` (1098) for focus; `1.4.2` (1137),
    `2.2.2` (1169), `1.3.2` (1205) for visual. The 1.4.10 fallback is asserted in
    `tests/unit/test_issues_view.py:629-657`.
  - Other axe rules (for example `meta-viewport`, `avoid-inline-spacing`) have no
    card; the header says they still appear but flagged for human review (`audit_report.yaml:6-9`), and the row title falls back to axe's own `help` text (`issues.py:957-966`).

#### WCAG level targeting

- Target is WCAG 2.2 A and AA: matrix meta (`wcag_coverage.yaml:28-34`),
  `docs/coverage-tracker.md:6`, UI copy "WCAG 2.2. AA is what most policies
  require" (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/components/newScan/copy.ts:160`).
- The axe level is user-selectable A, AA (default) or AAA in CLI, web form and
  protected requests (`cli.py:312-318`; `server.py:150`, `:2909`; React
  `ChecksGroup.tsx:36-41`). The same value drives Alfa (`orchestrator.py:1339`).
- **AAA checks that exist**: only when the user picks AAA, axe adds
  `color-contrast-enhanced` (1.4.6), `identical-links-same-purpose` (2.4.9) and
  `meta-refresh-no-exceptions` (2.2.4, 3.2.5) (VERIFIED-RUN), and Alfa switches to
  `Conformance.isAAA()` (`runner.mjs:168-173`). None of the probes in this scope
  target AAA.
- **2.5.5 Target Size (Enhanced, 44 x 44) is not checked** by any scanner code
  (see main table). The 44 px rules in the repo apply to Axcess' own React UI
  (`src/audit/web/frontend/tailwind.config.ts:135-139`), and the app's own UI test
  uses the AAA tag pack (`tests/ui/test_accessibility_axe.py:1-13`, `:47-52`).
  That is a self-test of the product, not a scan check.
- Outside this scope but relevant: the default semantic (LLM) criteria include
  two AAA criteria, 2.4.9 and 2.4.10 (`orchestrator.py:226-229`).

#### Notes and discrepancies for the doc rewrite

1. **Lane vs matrix tension**: the matrix calls 1.4.4, 1.4.10 and 1.4.12
   `automated` with high confidence (`wcag_coverage.yaml:207-266`), but every
   responsive finding lands in the `expert_review` lane, not `likely_barrier`
   (`issues.py:764`, `:829-832`).
2. **axe best-practice rows are `likely_barrier`** in the Issues view
   (`issues.py:946-952` has no best-practice branch), labelled conformance `BP`
   (`issues.py:1179-1182`). The audit report export moves any non-`likely_barrier`,
   `BP`, or SC-less row to its best-practice section (`src/audit/exports/audit_report.py:382-384`).
3. **axe `tabindex` shows as SC 2.4.3 Level A**: axe tags it best-practice only
   (VERIFIED-RUN), but the YAML card sets `wcag_sc: "2.4.3"`, `wcag_level: "A"`
   (`audit_report.yaml:322-326`) and card values win (`issues.py:744-745`).
   VERIFIED-READ; not executed.
4. **Duplicate detections across pipelines** (same defect, two rows, different
   lanes): positive tabindex (axe `tabindex` vs `focus-order-positive-tabindex`);
   marquee (axe `marquee` vs `visual-motion-no-pause`); autoplay audio (axe
   `no-autoplay-audio` vs `visual-autoplay-audio-no-control`). Inferred from rule
   logic; not observed on a real page.
5. **2.5.3 over-claim**: the matrix says axe flags `label-content-name-mismatch`
   (`wcag_coverage.yaml:431-442`), but that rule is experimental and never runs
   (VERIFIED-RUN).
6. **Focus probe blind spot**: `preventScroll: true` plus the in-viewport centre
   test (`focus/probe.py:160-165`) means only elements whose centre is inside the
   initial 1440 x 900 viewport are tested for 2.4.11. VERIFIED-READ.
7. **Probe order** on each page: axe, keyboard, focus, visual, interaction, then
   responsive last because it resizes the viewport and injects CSS; viewport is
   restored to 1440 x 900 (`src/audit/crawler/js_fetcher.py:45`, `:300-379`;
   `responsive/probe.py:201-205`; asserted in `test_responsive_probe.py:76-81`).
8. **Impact values**: responsive always `serious` (`responsive/probe.py:244`,
   `:272`, `:310`); focus default `serious` (`focus/base.py:63`); visual default
   `serious`, sequence mapped from model confidence (`visual/probe.py:252-254`);
   Alfa always NULL (`alfa.py:85-90`).
9. **Protected (signed-in) scans** force focus on and visual off
   (`server.py:1227-1228`); the React login policy fixes the same
   (`scanPolicy.ts:139-148`).
10. **Doc drift**: `responsive/probe.py:3` says the three checks are "absent from
    axe-core", yet axe runs `meta-viewport` (1.4.4) and `avoid-inline-spacing`
    (1.4.12), which test different conditions. `axe.py:235` points to
    `make setup` for a bundle that is actually committed.

## Appendix D: AI vision, AI language, keyboard, and interaction checks

Source file: agent notes `checks_ai_interaction.md`, copied verbatim with headings demoted.

### Axcess checks: AI vision, AI language, keyboard, interaction, and other probes

Verified facts for a documentation rewrite. Source: `/home/user/axcess` at commit `38eae99` (2026-09-23), clean working tree. All paths are repo-relative; `path:line` cites the line(s) read.

**How these facts were checked:** I read the source and did not execute it. I ran no tests, crawls, or models. "Verified" below means "read in code at the cited line", not "observed at runtime". Items I could not confirm from code are marked **UNVERIFIED**.

#### Default-setting vocabulary used in the table

Defaults differ by entry point. The table uses these labels:

- **CLI**: `audit crawl`. Every `--skip-*` flag defaults to False, so each pipeline is ON unless skipped (`src/audit/cli.py:287-430`, `src/audit/cli.py:505-528`). These match the `CrawlConfig` dataclass defaults (`src/audit/crawler/orchestrator.py:122-280`).
- **Web public**: the React "New scan" form. `PUBLIC_DEFAULTS` sets `skip_vlm: true`, `skip_semantic: true`, `skip_visual: true` and leaves everything else on (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/components/newScan/scanPolicy.ts:64-89`). The form posts to `POST /api/scans` (`src/audit/web/server.py:1346`), which builds the config in `_build_crawl_config` (`src/audit/web/server.py:2876-2929`).
- **Web login**: `POST /api/local-login-scans` (`src/audit/web/server.py:1139`). Its request model defaults are at `src/audit/web/server.py:151-157`, and the config is built at `src/audit/web/server.py:1186-1240`.
- **UI toggle labels** quoted in the table are defined in `src/audit/web/frontend/src/components/newScan/copy.ts:82-122`.
- **Raw API**: a direct `POST /api/scans` that leaves out a `skip_*` field. The server reads each field as `bool(body.get(...))`, so an omitted field means the pipeline is ON (`src/audit/web/server.py:1430-1443`). **This is the opposite of the Web public UI defaults for VLM, semantic, and visual.**

#### Main table

| Layer | Check | What it catches (plain words) | How it works | WCAG SC (level) | Lane | Default on? / how enabled | Source path:line | Test/fixture path |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AI vision | Image of text: raster OCR stage | Pictures that contain words (banners, posters, infographics, logos with text). | Reads `<img>` `src`/`srcset` and `<picture><source>` `src`/`srcset`, keeping every srcset candidate. Skips `data:` and `javascript:` URLs. **Does not read CSS background images.** Downloads each image (25 MB cap). Runs Tesseract through pytesseract (language `eng`, 2 worker processes). Skips SVG and icon files. An image counts as a text candidate when mean word confidence is >= 60 and it has >= 3 words. | Feeds 1.4.5 (AA) or 1.1.1 (A) through the image lane table below. | See image lane table. | CLI: on (`--skip-ocr`). Web public: on (toggle "Read text inside images (OCR)"). Web login: off (`skip_ocr=True`; turning it on requires an acknowledgement). | `src/audit/extractor/html_images.py:1-8,64-70,73-81,161-168`; `src/audit/extractor/downloader.py:28`; `src/audit/extractor/pipeline.py:42,123-125,249-250,263-282`; `src/audit/analyzer/ocr/tesseract.py:33-78`; `src/audit/analyzer/ocr/base.py:24-26`; `src/audit/config.py:38-41`; `src/audit/crawler/orchestrator.py:122-127,451-458`; `src/audit/web/server.py:154,186-189` | `tests/integration/test_ocr_pipeline.py:76,103,121,131`; `tests/unit/test_ocr.py`; `tests/unit/test_html_images.py`; fixture `tests/fixtures/site/gallery.html`, `tests/fixtures/site/images/` |
| AI vision | Image of text: inline SVG `<text>` | Text drawn inside an inline `<svg>` that the browser renders as graphics. | Finds each top-level `<svg>` that has non-empty `<text>` and ignores `<title>`/`<desc>`. Stores it as an "image" with `has_svg_text=1` and no blob. **It is flagged immediately with no OCR and no VLM.** The alt context is `aria-label` or the first `<title>`. | No SC assigned, because it is always "unclassified" (see lane table). | expert_review (low confidence). It moves to informational if the alt/`aria-label`/`title` context matches the text. | On whenever image extraction runs. CLI: always. Web public: always (`image_extraction_enabled` is not overridden).
