#### Image lane and WCAG mapping (classification x alt adequacy)

The mapping logic is in `src/audit/web/issues.py:1009-1090`. The WCAG metadata comes from the `image_findings` block of `src/audit/rules/audit_report.yaml:703-915`, keyed `<classification>_<adequacy>`. The rules are:

- The adequacy is `adequate` -> lane `informational` and the SC is suppressed (`issues.py:1027,1029,1044`).
- The classification is missing (VLM off, VLM failed, or inline SVG) -> "unclassified", no SC, lane `expert_review`, confidence `low` (`issues.py:1019,1028-1029,1045`).
- Otherwise -> lane `expert_review`, confidence `medium`. The SC comes from the YAML key if one exists. If there is no key, the SC is null and the conformance label becomes `BP` (`issues.py:1022,1029-1031,1179-1182`).

| Classification | missing | inadequate | partial | adequate |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| essential | 1.4.5 AA, expert_review (`audit_report.yaml:705-709`) | 1.4.5 AA, expert_review (`:738-742`) | 1.4.5 AA, expert_review (`:767-771`) | informational, SC suppressed (YAML says 1.4.5 AA at `:794-798`, overridden by `issues.py:1029`) |
| informational | 1.1.1 A, expert_review (`:824-828`) | no SC ("BP"), expert_review | no SC ("BP"), expert_review | informational |
| logo | 1.1.1 A, expert_review (`:857-861`) | no SC ("BP"), expert_review | no SC ("BP"), expert_review | informational |
| decorative | 1.1.1 A, expert_review (`:882-886`) | no SC ("BP"), expert_review | no SC ("BP"), expert_review | informational |
| no_meaningful_text | no SC ("BP"), expert_review | no SC ("BP"), expert_review | no SC ("BP"), expert_review | informational |
| unclassified | no SC, expert_review, low | no SC, expert_review, low | no SC, expert_review, low | informational, low |

Lane-assignment test coverage: `tests/unit/test_issues_view.py:153-184` checks that axe rows are `likely_barrier`, image rows are `expert_review`, and an adequate unclassified image is informational.

##### Severity and priority for image findings (separate from the lane)

- Priority score = classification weight + adequacy weight + log1p(pages) + 1 if above the fold.
  - Classification weights: essential 4, informational 3, logo 1, decorative 1, no_meaningful_text 0, none 0.
  - Adequacy weights: missing 3, inadequate 2, partial 1, adequate 0.
- Severity from score: critical >= 8, major >= 5, minor >= 2, else info (`src/audit/synthesizer/priority.py:1-19,37-44,46-51,54-66,69-77`).
- Findings are written only for images that have inline SVG text or an OCR text candidate (`src/audit/synthesizer/findings.py:112-153`).
- Remediation hints come from the first matching `(classification, adequacy)` rule, with `*` as a wildcard fallback (`src/audit/synthesizer/rules.py:1-6,52-60`). `src/audit/rules/remediation.yaml` lists all 20 explicit pairs plus 4 wildcard rows (lines 7-153).
- **Caveat:** nothing in the crawl ever sets `above_fold`. `upsert_page_image` defaults it to False (`src/audit/db/repo.py:1122`), and both extractor calls omit it (`src/audit/extractor/pipeline.py:112-120,144-152`), so the +1 visibility weight is never applied to crawled findings.

#### Semantic analyzers: registry, models, enablement

- **Configured vs implemented.** The default `semantic_criteria` tuple has **11** SCs: 2.4.4, 2.4.9, 2.4.6, 2.4.10, 2.5.3, 3.3.2, 1.3.5, 1.3.1, 4.1.2, 1.1.1, 1.2.1 (`src/audit/crawler/orchestrator.py:224-236`). Only **4** have analyzer classes: 1.2.1, 2.4.4, 2.4.6, 3.3.2 (`src/audit/analyzer/semantic/registry.py:34-39`). The other 7 log `semantic.unknown_criterion` and are skipped (`registry.py:76-83`). The CLI help text says "default set is 10 criteria" (`src/audit/cli.py:324-326`), which matches neither number.
- **Model resolution.**
  - The shared provider and its health check use the text default `gemma2:9b` (`src/audit/rules/analyzer_models.yaml:43`; `src/audit/crawler/orchestrator.py:1086-1103`).
  - Each analyzer then calls its per-SC pick (`registry.py:86-87`): 2.4.4 uses `gemma2:9b` (`analyzer_models.yaml:116-119`); 1.2.1, 2.4.6, and 3.3.2 use `qwen2.5:7b-instruct` (`:106-109,129-132,154-157`).
  - The YAML comments say the `gemma2:9b` pin is a temporary demo pin (`:39-42,123-127`).
  - The web capability endpoint `GET /api/capabilities/local-analysis` reports semantic as available only when every per-SC model is installed (`src/audit/web/server.py:1033,1066-1110`).
- **Failure behavior.**
  - If `gemma2:9b` is missing or Ollama is unreachable, the whole semantic pass is skipped with a warning (`orchestrator.py:1093-1103`).
  - If only `qwen2.5:7b-instruct` is missing, each of those 3 analyzers catches `OllamaError` per page and returns no findings (`sc_1_2_1.py:75`, `sc_2_4_6.py:75`, `sc_3_3_2.py:72`; Ollama 4xx raises at `ollama_base.py:147-149`). This is inferred from the code path and not run.
- **Transport.** Ollama `/api/generate`, temperature 0, `format=json`, `keep_alive=-1` (the model stays resident), 120 s timeout, 3 attempts (`src/audit/analyzer/semantic/ollama_text.py:45-69,95-104`). Analyzers run concurrently per page, throttled by one shared semaphore (default concurrency 1) (`src/audit/analyzer/semantic/runner.py:35-73`; `orchestrator.py:237`).
- **Input.** Analyzers work on the page's HTML body, which is the rendered DOM when the page was JS-fetched. There is no live page handle (`page=None`), so **semantic findings never get element screenshots** (`orchestrator.py:2133-2141,2158-2161`). The pass runs on static and rendered fetches alike (`orchestrator.py:1451-1457`).
- **Storage.** Rows are stored as `rule_id = "semantic:<sc>"`, `pipeline='semantic'`, with `wcag_sc` = the criterion (`src/audit/db/repo.py:868,883,906-912`).
- **Enablement surfaces.**
  - CLI: `--skip-semantic` and `--semantic-criteria 2.4.4,...` (`cli.py:319-340,541-549`).
  - Web public: toggle "Review wording with local AI", default off (`scanPolicy.ts:84`; `copy.ts:114-118`).
  - Web login: forced off (`server.py:1214`; `scanPolicy.ts:145`).
- **Lane.** Semantic rows default to `expert_review`, medium confidence, with the summary "AI-assisted semantic lead; confirm in page context" (`src/audit/web/issues.py:764-772`).
- **Card copy.** Only 2.4.4 has a `semantic_criteria` metadata card (`audit_report.yaml:967-971`). For 1.2.1, 2.4.6, and 3.3.2 the issue title falls back to `"WCAG SC <sc> (LLM-detected)"` with no description or fix steps (`issues.py:726-728,771,957-966`). This is inferred from the lookup logic.

#### Interaction probe: details

- **What it clicks.** It considers the elements matched by the `_CANDIDATE_SELECTOR` list (`probe.py:121-127`). It excludes a candidate that is any of the following (`probe.py:132-161`):
  - invisible, inside `[inert]`/`[aria-hidden=true]`, disabled, or `aria-disabled`;
  - a real link (href not `""` or `#`) or a `download` anchor, since links are left to the crawler;
  - a widget container role (listbox/menu/menubar/tablist/tree/grid);
  - a `select`, or an input of type file/submit/reset/password/hidden. The one exception is a `<button type=submit>` outside any form.
- **Label blocklist.** It never operates a control whose label + title + name + id + formaction contains a blocked word, matched case-insensitively as a substring. The words include sign out, logout, delete, remove, unsubscribe, subscribe, purchase, buy, pay, checkout, add to cart, register, sign up, submit, save, send, publish, upload, download, confirm, approve, agree, accept all, upgrade, donate, transfer, and others (`probe.py:60-117,184-185,725-729`). The crawl's URL blocklist is appended: `/logout`, `/delete`, `/remove`, `/signout`, `/sign-out`, `/log-out` (`orchestrator.py:542`; `src/audit/crawler/url_policy.py:39-46`).
- **Network guard (`safety.py`).** For the whole sweep, a context-wide route does the following (`src/audit/analyzer/interaction/safety.py:46-154`):
  - blocks any request that is a navigation, a popup, a non-GET/HEAD/OPTIONS method, cross-origin, or a URL containing a blocked word;
  - answers blocked navigations with an empty 204 so the DOM survives;
  - dismisses JS `alert`/`confirm`/`prompt` dialogs;
  - refuses to run at all if service workers are present;
  - keeps blocked GET navigation targets (up to 1000) and hands them to the crawler, which still applies scope checks.
  - The module states it is "defense in depth, not a transaction sandbox: sites can mutate state through GET or pre-existing sockets" (`safety.py:1-6`).
- **Limits.**

  | Limit | Value | Source |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Clicks per page | 100 | `probe.py:299` |
  | Controls sampled per structural shape (digits collapsed, so a 365-cell calendar is one shape) | 20 | `probe.py:300,350-363` |
  | Nesting depth | 5 | `probe.py:301` |
  | Wall time per page | 120 s | `probe.py:316,497` |
  | Per-click timeout | 3 s | `probe.py:775` |
  | Settle wait after each click | 400 ms | `probe.py:460` |
  | Inert-shape sampling (stop a shape after this many no-op clicks, only for shapes with >= 8 members) | 3 | `probe.py:302-307,635-642` |
  | Candidates collected per sweep | 2000 | `probe.py:193` |
  | State captures per page | 25, at <= 2 MB each and 8 MB total | `probe.py:326-329` |
  | State screenshots per page | 25, 12 MB total | `probe.py:311-313` |

  CLI and web use the dataclass defaults of 100/20/5 (`orchestrator.py:278-280`). **UNVERIFIED:** I found no CLI flag that changes these three limits (no `--interaction-max-*` option in `cli.py:280-440`).
- **Dialogs.**
  - Modal detection uses `aria-modal=true` or a native `<dialog>` matching `:modal`. A plain `role=dialog` is not treated as modal (`probe.py:206-255`).
  - A modal that a click opened is closed with Escape. If that fails, it uses the dialog's own close control, but only if that control is named close, cancel, dismiss, no thanks, not now, go back, or a close glyph. "OK", "Done", and "Continue" are deliberately excluded (`probe.py:257-272,912-957`).
  - If the dialog stays open, the page's exploration halts. The limit `dialog_not_dismissed` and a <= 200-char detail are recorded (`probe.py:958-975`).
- **Checks re-run in a revealed state.** Only axe-core, at the crawl's axe level (`probe.py:977-998`). **Keyboard, focus, responsive, visual, and semantic checks do not re-run in revealed states.** The JS fetcher calls those probes once on the load state (`src/audit/crawler/js_fetcher.py:312-379`).
- **Dedupe.**
  - The load-state axe violations are the baseline, so anything seen at load is never re-reported (`probe.py:483-492`; `js_fetcher.py:359-361`).
  - Findings are upserted through the ordinary axe path. The UNIQUE key `(page_id, rule_id, target_hash)` is the backstop, and `revealed_by` is excluded from the hash (`src/audit/analyzer/interaction/base.py:7-16,35-39`; `orchestrator.py:1834-1841`).
- **Recording.**
  - Each revealed finding stores `revealed_by`, the accessible name of the operated control (label <= 300 chars), and `revealed_state_key` (`scope|selector|label`) (`base.py:31-51`; `orchestrator.py:1886-1887`).
  - For a state that held a new defect, the gzipped DOM plus the ordered `path_labels` chain of clicked controls is stored via `replace_page_dom_states` (`base.py:65-91`; `probe.py:843-855`; `orchestrator.py:1851-1858`).
  - A per-page ledger records controls found/attempted/succeeded/operated, states, blocked controls, limits hit, dialogs opened/stuck, and detail (`orchestrator.py:1424-1443`).
  - Migrations: `0022_interaction_revealed_by`, `0024_scan_interaction_states`, `0026_interaction_runs` in `src/audit/db/migrations/`.
  - Element screenshots are taken while the revealed state is still open (`probe.py:1000-1045`).
- **How it is shown.**
  - Issue evidence shows "After clicking “<control>”" (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/components/IssueEvidence.tsx:228-230`).
  - Page evidence groups findings by revealing control, separate from "at load" findings (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/routes/PageEvidence.tsx:75,365-369`).
  - The Inspector shows the revealed state, and its wording is "first flagged after activating" rather than blaming site drift (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/routes/Inspector.tsx:115,312,584`; `tests/ui/test_inspector_revealed_state.py:81-99`).
  - The coverage panel entry is "Click Through DOM States" (`src/audit/web/server.py:3705-3725`).
- **Navigation.** A click that navigates is undone (go back, else goto), and the new URL is queued for the crawler (`probe.py:786-800,1105-1117`).

#### Keyboard probe: what it does NOT detect

- The module docstring says it does not detect:
  - traps that only appear after interaction;
  - traps inside closed shadow DOM or embedded browsing contexts;
  - pages behind login;
  - screen-reader virtual-cursor traps;
  - components with a documented non-standard exit command (`src/audit/analyzer/keyboard/probe.py:19-27`).
  - Note (inference): "pages behind login" looks stale, because Web login scans enable the probe (`src/audit/web/server.py:1225`).
- It does **not** check focus order, unreachable controls, or focus visibility. Positive tabindex (2.4.3) belongs to the focus probe (`src/audit/analyzer/focus/base.py:17`). Grepping `src/audit/analyzer` for reachability checks found none, apart from the legacy iframe-title helper below.
- The Escape-key modal check and the untitled-iframe check are retained as legacy helpers but are **not called** by `run()` (`probe.py:13-17,97-109,231-238`). Rules `keyboard-trap-modal-no-escape` and `keyboard-trap-iframe` are "no longer emitted" (`src/audit/analyzer/keyboard/base.py:19-20`). Older scans holding them, or holding one-direction `keyboard-trap-stuck` rows, are shown as `informational`, low confidence, no SC (`src/audit/web/issues.py:708-717,739-751,797-822`; test `tests/unit/test_issues_view.py:468`).

#### Known caveats and doc mismatches found in code

1. **UI copy overstates the VLM's job.** The web toggle says the vision model "Judges whether an image's alt text matches what it shows" (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/components/newScan/copy.ts:109-113`). In code, the VLM only classifies the image (`classify_v1.txt:4-20,27-31`). Alt matching is a rapidfuzz string comparison (`src/audit/synthesizer/alt_compare.py:43-70`).
2. **UI copy mentions flashing.** The "Check motion and animation" hint says it "Flags flashing" (`copy.ts:119-122`). Grepping `src/audit/analyzer` and `src/audit/crawler` for "flash" finds nothing, and no 2.3.1 detector exists.
3. **Coverage-panel copy for the keyboard probe is stale.** It says the probe "tests Escape behavior" (`src/audit/web/server.py:3684-3686`), but `run()` only does the Tab/Shift+Tab walk (`keyboard/probe.py:97-109`).
4. **The image method's "enabled" flag needs both OCR and VLM** (`server.py:3647`). A default Web public scan runs OCR with VLM off, so it records unclassified image findings while the coverage panel reports the image method as not enabled. This is inferred from the two defaults and not run.
5. **A default Web public scan yields only "unclassified" image leads.** VLM is off by default (`scanPolicy.ts:77`), and unclassified groups get no SC (`issues.py:1028-1029`). To get 1.4.5/1.1.1 mapping, the operator must enable the vision model.
6. **Where the vision model name comes from.** The image and visual pipelines use `AUDIT_VLM_MODEL` (`config.py:45`; `orchestrator.py:1010-1016,1037-1041`), not `analyzer_models.yaml`. That YAML's vision entries (`:25-32,75-104`) are not read by any pipeline: `get_pick` is only called for semantic/text (`orchestrator.py:1086`, `registry.py:86`, `server.py:1068`).
7. **Stale comments.** The orchestrator comment calls the interaction probe "opt-in" (`orchestrator.py:521`), but the default is True (`orchestrator.py:277`). The server comment says semantic wiring "lands in Phase 9.1, for now this just plumbs the flag" (`server.py:2919-2922`), but the semantic pass is wired end to end (`orchestrator.py:1456-1457`).
8. **The Semantic coverage counter may overstate coverage (inference).** It increments whenever analyzers are configured, even if every model call failed and returned [] (`orchestrator.py:2180-2185`).
9. **The Tesseract binary is required but its absence is quiet.** If it is missing, `run_tesseract` raises inside `_ocr_image`, which catches everything and marks the image as failed. The page continues with no image findings (`pipeline.py:263-270`). The web capability endpoint reports `ocr.available` from `shutil.which("tesseract")` (`server.py:1075`).
10. **Image scope limits.** Only `img` and `picture > source` are read, with no CSS `background-image`, `data:` URIs, or `<canvas>` (`html_images.py:64-70,161-168`). SVG files and icons are downloaded but not OCR'd (`pipeline.py:42,249-250`). An inline SVG image is never VLM-classified (`pipeline.py:127-153`).
11. **2.4.4 is stricter than the other semantic analyzers.** It drops low-confidence model flags in code (`sc_2_4_4.py:177-188`). 2.4.6, 3.3.2, and 1.2.1 keep them at impact `minor`, and the 1.2.1 prompt actively asks for low-confidence flags when unsure (`sc_1_2_1_audio_transcript.txt:16-17`).
12. **Per-call caps drop the rest of the page silently.** Only a log line records it: 50 links, 60 headings, 50 fields, 40 audio elements (`sc_2_4_4.py:41,82-89`; `sc_2_4_6.py:32,63-69`; `sc_3_3_2.py:29,60-66`; `sc_1_2_1.py:31,63-69`).
13. **A stray `.bak` file is tracked.** `src/audit/analyzer/semantic/analyzers/sc_2_4_4.py.bak` is tracked in git and not imported (`src/audit/analyzer/semantic/analyzers/__init__.py:11-29` imports only sc_1_2_1, sc_2_4_4, sc_2_4_6, and sc_3_3_2).
14. **Coverage tracker omissions.** `src/audit/web/coverage_status.py:54-120` (`SHIPPED`) lists axe, keyboard, responsive, focus, image, visual, and semantic. It does not list the interaction probe or Alfa.
15. **Reviewer note (WCAG knowledge, not code; please confirm).** `audit_report.yaml:804-807` says for `essential_adequate` that "AA compliance is achieved by adequate alt". In WCAG, adequate alt text addresses 1.1.1 and does not by itself satisfy 1.4.5. Also, Axcess's "essential" label (prompt `classify_v1.txt:6-9`: text that should become real text) is close to the opposite of WCAG 1.4.5's "essential" exception (a presentation that must stay an image). Docs should define the Axcess label explicitly.

#### Verified vs assumed summary

- **Verified by reading code:** every `path:line` citation above.
- **Assumed or inferred and not run:** runtime outcomes of missing models (caveat 4, the semantic missing-model path), coverage-counter overstatement (caveat 8), stale docstring about login (keyboard section), and which test files exercise Alfa, search, and protected-image rows (marked UNVERIFIED in the table).
- **Not executed:** no tests, crawls, or Ollama calls.

## Appendix E: Crawl behavior, data and privacy, installation

Source file: agent notes `crawl_data.md`, copied verbatim with headings demoted.

### Axcess: crawl behavior, data and privacy, installation (verified facts)

Method: read-only source reading at /home/user/axcess (git status clean at start). No tests, servers, crawls or builds were run. "Verified" below means "read in the cited code or test"; nothing was executed. Items marked UNVERIFIED could not be confirmed from code. Items marked DISCREPANCY are places where a doc or UI string contradicts the code.

Three scan entry points use different defaults. Keep them apart in the docs:
- **UI (New scan form):** `src/audit/web/frontend/src/components/newScan/scanPolicy.ts`
- **HTTP API `POST /api/scans`** when a field is omitted: `src/audit/web/server.py:1420-1446`
- **CLI `audit crawl`:** `src/audit/cli.py:230-529`

---

#### A. Crawl behavior

##### A1. Scope, limits, robots, sitemap, rate, rendering, SPA

**Scope**
- Scope is the seed URL's host plus its path prefix. `https://x/section/` crawls only `/section/*`. A seed with no trailing slash and no file extension gets a slash added. A file-like seed (`/docs/intro.html`) is scoped to its directory. A bare host means the whole host. (`src/audit/crawler/url_policy.py:6-16`, `:148-169`, `:172-186`, `:189-203`)
- `www.` and the apex domain count as the same host (`url_policy.py:266-267`, `:281`).
- Subdomains are opt-in (`allow_subdomains`, which matches on the registrable domain) (`url_policy.py:278-279`).
- Only `http` and `https` are followed (`url_policy.py:273-274`).
- "Whole host" ignores the path prefix (`url_policy.py:192-198`). The UI switch is "Crawl the entire host" (`newScan/copy.ts:65-68`).
- The scope check runs twice: when a link is queued (`src/audit/crawler/orchestrator.py:1572-1576`) and again when the job is leased (`orchestrator.py:1153-1156`).
- Out-of-scope links found on a page are dropped with a debug log. They are not counted (`orchestrator.py:1575`). Only jobs rejected at lease time increment `pages_skipped_scope` (`orchestrator.py:1154`).

**Link discovery**
- New pages come from `<a href>` in the fetched (or rendered) HTML, plus up to 1000 extra URLs found by the interaction probe and the configured search (`orchestrator.py:1560-1561`; `src/audit/crawler/js_fetcher.py:461-463`).
- Forms, iframes and script-only navigation are not parsed as links. This is inferred from the code: only `a[href]` is selected at `orchestrator.py:1560`.

**Blocked URLs and exclusions**
- Built-in blocklist of URL substrings, case-insensitive: `/logout`, `/delete`, `/remove`, `/signout`, `/sign-out`, `/log-out` (`url_policy.py:39-46`, `:49-57`).
- Operator exclusions ("never visit") match as an exact URL or a prefix (`url_policy.py:60-75`).
- The CLI adds `--block`, `--exclude` and `--allow-session-ending-urls` (`cli.py:260-286`, `:497-502`).
- The web form and API expose neither blocklist nor exclusions. `_build_crawl_config` never sets them, so the defaults apply (`server.py:2876-2929`).

**Page limits and depth**
- `max_pages` defaults: CrawlConfig 500 (`orchestrator.py:103`), CLI 500 (`cli.py:233-235`), UI 2500 (`scanPolicy.ts:66`), API 2500 when omitted (`server.py:1423`).
- The UI caps public scans at 10000 pages and depth 20 (`scanPolicy.ts:115`).
- `max_depth` defaults to 10 everywhere (`orchestrator.py:104`, `cli.py:236-238`, `scanPolicy.ts:67`, `server.py:1424`).
- The seed is depth 0 (`orchestrator.py:867`). Children are queued only while `depth < max_depth` (`orchestrator.py:1461-1462`).
- The page limit counts pages with a fetch result (`orchestrator.py:1253`, `:1142-1143`). Network failures (FetchError) do not count toward it. They are recorded as a page row with a null status and counted as errors (`orchestrator.py:1197-1201`, `:1233-1237`).

**robots.txt**
- Respected by default (`ignore_robots=False` at `orchestrator.py:107`; UI `scanPolicy.ts:72`; CLI `cli.py:256-259`).
- Fetch rules: a network error allows all, a 4xx allows all, a 5xx disallows all. Results are cached per origin for 1 hour (`src/audit/crawler/robots.py:1-6`, `:55-65`, `:96-112`).
- The check runs per job (`orchestrator.py:1168-1171`).
- The UI switch is "Ignore the site's robots.txt rules", with the hint "Authorized testing only; the scan is flagged in its config and audit log." (`copy.ts:77-81`). `ignore_robots` is saved in `config_json` (`orchestrator.py:773`).
- Robots `Crawl-delay` is parsed (`robots.py:82-90`), but nothing calls `crawl_delay()`. Verified by grep: the only occurrence is its own definition.
- `Retry-After` is parsed into `FetchResult.retry_after` (`src/audit/crawler/fetcher.py:134`), but the orchestrator never reads it (grep).
- DISCREPANCY: login scans always ignore robots.txt (`server.py:1240`; `scanPolicy.ts:99`, `:142`), but the login form's fixed note says the scan "respects robots.txt" (`copy.ts:180-181`). The summary card says "Stays on this website." for login instead of a robots line (`ScanSummaryCard.tsx:68`).

**Sitemaps**
- Not used. `src/audit/crawler/sitemap.py` exists (`:1-6`), but no production code imports `urls_from_sitemap` or `discover_sitemaps`; only `tests/unit/test_sitemap.py:9` does (grep).
- `RobotsInfo.sitemaps` is collected (`robots.py:116`) but never consumed.

**Rate limiting and concurrency**
- Per-host token bucket (`rps`) plus a per-host semaphore (`concurrency_per_host`, default 2) (`src/audit/crawler/rate_limit.py:249-282`; `orchestrator.py:106`, `:118`, `:441`).
- Defaults: rps 2.0 in CrawlConfig, CLI, UI and API (`orchestrator.py:106`, `cli.py:253-255`, `scanPolicy.ts:68`, `server.py:1425`).
- Workers: CrawlConfig 4 (`orchestrator.py:119`, which the CLI uses), UI 8 (`scanPolicy.ts:69`), API 8 (`server.py:1426`). The UI caps public scans at rps 50 and 32 workers (`scanPolicy.ts:115`).
- The web form does not set `concurrency_per_host`, so it stays at 2 (`server.py:2881-2929`).
- Only the static HTTP fetch is paced. On the default public path (static fetch, then browser render) the render sits outside the throttle (`orchestrator.py:1204-1238` throttle block; `:1242-1248` escalation outside it; rationale at `:173-185`, `:1181-1185`).
- Browser-only (login) fetches run inside the throttle (`orchestrator.py:1204-1212`).
- Request timeout is 30 s (`src/audit/config.py:34`). The HTTP client uses HTTP/2 and allows at most 10 redirects (`orchestrator.py:660-666`).
- User agent: `axcess/0.1 (+local accessibility audit)` (`config.py:35`).

**JavaScript rendering**
- Default (`js_eager=True`): every HTML page is fetched statically, then re-rendered in Playwright Chromium (`orchestrator.py:141-152`, `:1703-1715`).
- Non-HTML responses (PDFs, images) are not rendered (`orchestrator.py:1714`).
- "Fast crawl" (UI) or `--static-only` (CLI) renders only pages that look like an SPA shell or a bot challenge (`orchestrator.py:1716-1719`; heuristics in `src/audit/crawler/render_detect.py:305-352`, `:358-384`).
- Axe, keyboard, focus, responsive, visual and interaction checks run only on browser-rendered pages (`orchestrator.py:1325`, `:1366`, `:1377`, `:1387`, `:1396`, `:1409-1413`).
- The API refuses static-only mode combined with axe (`server.py:1411-1418`).
- `skip_static_when_rendering` (default off) skips the duplicate static fetch (`orchestrator.py:164-188`, `:1678-1700`).
- Public-scan rendering details:
  - A fresh BrowserContext per page, so cookies do not carry between pages (`js_fetcher.py:3-6`, `:242-248`).
  - Service workers blocked and downloads off (`js_fetcher.py:245-246`).
  - Viewport 1440x900 (`js_fetcher.py:45`).
  - Navigation timeout 30 s (`js_fetcher.py:46`).
  - Network-idle wait of 2.5 s by default (`orchestrator.py:163`).
  - Headless by default. "Show the scanning browser window" opts into a visible browser (`orchestrator.py:191`; `copy.ts:129-133`).

**SPA and hash routes**
- URL normalization keeps `#/route` and `#!/route` fragments and drops ordinary `#anchors` (`url_policy.py:86-87`, `:113-123`).
- The rendered page identity uses `page.url`, which keeps hash routes and client-side redirects (`js_fetcher.py:295-299`).
- A sign-in landing URL keeps its exact fragment for the entry point only (`orchestrator.py:637-657`).
- Tests cover hash, hashbang and history routers after login (`tests/integration/test_authenticated_spa.py:164-267`).

**Other behavior**
- Resumable: an interrupted crawl of the same seed reuses the row and drains the SQLite job queue (`orchestrator.py:1-6`, `:683-746`).
- Stopping a scan from the UI clears its queue, so a stopped scan is not resumed (`server.py:1457-1491`).
- One crawl at a time per server process: HTTP 409 "A crawl is already running." (`server.py:1356-1365`).

##### A2. Authenticated scanning ("Site with a login or 2FA")

There are two distinct mechanisms. Document them separately.

**(1) Local login scan.** This is the practical path, and it is what the desktop app offers.

UI and entry:
- Tab label "Site with a login or 2FA" (`copy.ts:14`; `ScanTypeTabs.tsx:10-13`).
- URL label "Page to scan after you sign in" (`copy.ts:23-28`).
- Submit "Open browser to sign in" (`copy.ts:200-204`).
- Endpoint `POST /api/local-login-scans` (`server.py:1139-1285`).

How it works:
- The user signs in themselves in a real, visible Chromium window. `ManualAuthenticationSession.start()` launches a headed persistent context (`headless=False`) in a fresh temporary profile directory and opens the seed URL (`src/audit/protected/session.py:434-484`).
- UI status while waiting: "Sign in in the Chromium window" / "Complete the full login and 2FA flow, then return here." (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/components/LocalLoginScan.tsx:94-97`).
- The user presses "I'm signed in, start scan" (`LocalLoginScan.tsx:186-194`), which calls `POST /api/local-login-scans/{id}/confirm` (`server.py:1327-1344`).
- After confirmation the crawl starts from where sign-in landed. Scope still comes from the typed seed (`server.py:3043-3051`; `orchestrator.py:87-102`, `:403-420`).
- Pop-up and SSO tabs opened during sign-in are adopted rather than closed (`session.py:231-260`, `:491-517`).

Restrictions and fixed settings:
- Available only when the browser talks to Axcess over loopback with a matching Origin. Otherwise HTTP 403 "Direct login scanning is restricted to this Axcess computer." (`server.py:3273-3314`). The capability reason text is at `server.py:3281`.
- Seed must be HTTPS with no credentials, query or fragment (`server.py:177-185`; UI `scanPolicy.ts:156-165`).
- The seed host must be a DNS name, not an IP, `localhost` or `.local` (`src/audit/protected/models.py:69-109`).
- Every request must resolve to a globally routable (public) address (`src/audit/protected/egress.py:760-782`; test `tests/unit/test_protected_session.py:104-120`). So internal-only or private-IP intranet apps cannot be login-scanned. The intranet consequence is inferred from `is_global`, not tested against a real intranet.
- Limits: `max_pages` up to 2500, depth up to 20, rps 0.1 to 5 (default 1.0), workers 1 to 4 (default 2) (`server.py:143-146`; UI `scanPolicy.ts:92-103`, `:137`).
- Fixed values: `ignore_robots=True`, `browser_only=True`, semantic off, visual off, focus on, `resumable=False` (`server.py:1214-1246`).
- Every login scan creates a new report and cannot be resumed (`server.py:1248-1251`).
- DISCREPANCY: the fixed note says it "runs at 1 request per second" (`copy.ts:180-181`), but rps is editable up to 5 in the login form (`SpeedGroup.tsx:21-30` with `LOGIN_POLICY.caps.rps=5` at `scanPolicy.ts:137`).

Two-factor authentication (2FA, Duo, MFA):
- Supported by design, because the human completes every factor in the headed browser. Axcess does not automate or handle any factor.
- Capability endpoint returns `"authentication": "manual"`, `"supported_sign_in": ["password", "mfa"]` (`server.py:1129-1130`; asserted in `tests/ui/test_protected_routes.py:268-269`, `:286-287`).
- The local flow passes `allow_any_public_auth_origin=True`, so dynamically assigned IdP or MFA hosts are reachable during sign-in (`server.py:1256-1265`; `session.py:132-136`; `egress.py:259-268`).
- Unit test: a Duo-style host (`api-12345.duosecurity.com`) is accepted during sign-in (`tests/unit/test_protected_session.py:123-153`).
- UI hint: "Make sure the visible browser shows the protected application, not the U-M or Duo login screen." (`LocalLoginScan.tsx:164-167`).
- UNVERIFIED: an end-to-end run against a real MFA challenge. No test performs one. Integration tests simulate sign-in with a fixture "Sign in" button and cookie or sessionStorage, and state "No network listener or real identity provider is needed" (`tests/integration/test_authenticated_spa.py:130-161`, `:176-177`, `:194`). `tests/fixtures/site/authed/*` is a static app with a Sign out link, used to test the logout blocklist (`tests/integration/test_crawl_end_to_end.py:600-628`). It has no login or 2FA step.

Credentials, cookies and session handling:
- The request model has no credential fields and forbids extra fields (`server.py:135-158`).
- UI copy: "your password is typed into the site, never into Axcess. The session stays in memory and is destroyed when the scan ends." (`copy.ts:183-188`).
- Docstring: the report stores rendered evidence but "never a password, OTP, cookie, authorization header, browser profile, or reusable storage-state object" (`server.py:3028-3034`).
- Session reuse inside one scan:
  - **Default, browser hidden.** Cookies, localStorage, IndexedDB and tab sessionStorage are captured in memory with `storage_state(indexed_db=True)` and loaded into a new headless Chromium. The sign-in window is then closed (`session.py:535-583`; `server.py:3052-3054`). Tested: `tests/integration/test_authenticated_spa.py:21-24`.
  - **"Show browser".** The scan reuses the signed-in context's tabs (`session.py:590-626`; `server.py:3055-3060`).
  - If the site keeps its session in sessionStorage, a single tab is reused and access is serialized (`session.py:573`, `:608-616`).
- Clearing: `run.session.close()` always runs at the end (`server.py:3175-3178`). It closes the context, browser and Playwright, stops the egress proxy, and deletes the temporary profile directory (`session.py:830-857`).
- Profile directory: `<OS temp>/axcess-protected-browser/axcess-protected-browser-*`, mode 0700. Stale directories are removed on the next start (`session.py:872-898`).
- Chromium may write cache or profile data into that temporary directory while it runs. Documented at `docs/protected-scans.md:68-85` and `session.py:875-879`.
- After an Axcess restart the status becomes interrupted: "The in-memory login browser is no longer available. Start a new login scan." (`server.py:1303-1325`).
- `make run` uses `--reload`. `make run-stable` exists "safe for long login scans" (`Makefile:48-52`).

Network policy after sign-in:
- Scan mode allows any public HTTPS destination (no allowlist and no GET/HEAD-only rule). It still refuses plaintext, userinfo and private or loopback addresses (`session.py:137-149`, `:339-351`).
- Page crawl scope still limits which documents are visited (A1).
- DISCREPANCY (internal): the `PublicHttpsManualAuthPolicy` docstring says the browser "switches irreversibly to an exact-origin ProtectedEgressPolicy" before crawling (`egress.py:266-267`). The code builds `scan_policy = PublicHttpsManualAuthPolicy` instead (`session.py:149`).

What a login scan stores:
- It is an ordinary `scans` row, not a protected report. No `protected_scans` row is created on this path (`server.py:1186-1285`).
- By default it stores rendered HTML and circled element screenshots of signed-in pages in the normal local database and blob store (`server.py:1229-1239`). The "Don't store rendered pages" switch turns both off (test `tests/ui/test_routes.py:448-494`).
- Image OCR is off by default. Turning it on requires the "Store protected image-analysis evidence locally" acknowledgement (`server.py:154-157`, `:186-189`; `copy.ts:190-196`; `NewScan.tsx:215-231`).
- Vision-model analysis in login scans requires Ollama on a literal loopback address (`server.py:1163-1173`, `:3227-3257`).
- The encryption, 7-day retention and redaction below do NOT apply to local login scans. Inferred from the absence of any protected-row creation on this path.
- Logs: each page's requested URL and title are logged with no protected-context condition (`orchestrator.py:1175`, `:1278-1287`). Logs go to `data/logs/axcess.log` (see B1). So signed-in URLs and titles reach the log. Verified by reading only.

Failure detection:
- Sign-in pages are detected by an auth-like path segment (`/login`, `/sign-in`, `/signin`, `/sso`, `/mfa`, `/verify`) or a password, one-time-code or WebAuthn input (`render_detect.py:387-419`).
- The scan is marked failed if zero pages were scanned, or if every scanned page was a sign-in page (`server.py:3196-3224`).
- A session that expires partway through is not detected. Such scans can finish "completed" with some login-page captures (inferred from `server.py:3210`, which checks `>=` all pages; no mid-scan stop exists in this path).

**(2) Protected scans (companion).**

A separate, hosted, disabled-by-default deployment feature: `AUDIT_PROTECTED_SCANS_ENABLED=false` (`config.py:57-62`; `docs/protected-scans.md:87-91`).

Requirements:
- A signed identity-aware proxy, a companion mTLS proxy, a public origin, and a vault with irreversible per-scan key destruction (`server.py:461-491`).
- The auditor runs `axcess-companion pair/run` or `audit protected-companion pair/run` (`docs/protected-scans.md:248-266`; `cli.py:56`, `:75-129`).

What a "protected report" is:
- A `scans` row with a `protected_scans` row (`orchestrator.py:710-712`).
- Only an opaque, redacted issue index returns. No URLs, selectors, screenshots or raw HTML (`docs/protected-scans.md:33-38`).
- The companion crawls with `capture_screenshots=False` and a rendered-HTML size cap (`src/audit/protected/companion.py:557-558`).
- It validates every page against the approved target origins (`companion.py:699-702`).
- A 401, 403 or auth-page response stops the scan as authentication required (`companion.py:598`, `:337-346`).

What is encrypted:
- AES-GCM envelope encryption with a KMS-wrapped per-scan data key (`src/audit/protected/crypto.py:1-6`, `:30-60`).
- Covers the work specification (exact origins) and artifacts (`crypto.py:177-229`; `repository.py:140`, `:1035`).

Retention:
- 7 days (`src/audit/protected/retention.py:7-15`).
- Cleanup via `audit protected-maintenance` or `make protected-maintenance` (`Makefile:66-67`; `cli.py:179-180`).

Redaction:
- Best-effort regexes for tokens, cookies, auth headers, emails, SSNs and phone numbers (`src/audit/protected/redaction.py:1-40`).
- Redacted summary export: `POST /api/protected-scans/{id}/exports/redacted` (`src/audit/web/protected_api.py:546`).

Other checks:
- Deleting a protected report revokes its KMS key first (`server.py:1528-1561`).
- DISCREPANCY: `docs/protected-scans.md:317-320` says that after sign-in the crawl permits only GET/HEAD to the target and approved CDNs. The shared session code no longer enforces GET/HEAD or an allowlist in scan mode (`session.py:137-149`, `:339-351`). The companion still validates page URLs against target origins (`companion.py:699-702`, `:761`). The comment at `companion.py:334-335` still says "GET/HEAD-only".
- DISCREPANCY: `docs/protected-scans.md:272` says the companion "verifies the returned application origin". `enter_scan_mode()` now accepts wherever sign-in landed without checking it (`session.py:519-533`). Page validation happens later, per page (`companion.py:699-702`).

##### A3. Search-driven pages

UI location and gating:
- Checkbox "Search to discover result pages" (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/components/SearchSettings.tsx:45`).
- It sits in the "Checks" group under Advanced settings (`newScan/ChecksGroup.tsx:91-97`; `ScanForm.tsx:129-136`).
- DISCREPANCY: `docs/spa-search-scans.md:39-41` says "New scan > Advanced settings > Search-driven pages" and "Login or 2FA website". The UI group is "Checks" and the tab is "Site with a login or 2FA".
- Requires browser rendering and axe-core. The API refuses static-only mode and Alfa-only (`server.py:520-550`; UI `SearchSettings.tsx:48`).

What it types:
- Only the values the operator enters: 1 to 6 fields, each value at most 200 chars, matched by accessible label or CSS selector, as a text fill or a select-option-by-label (`src/audit/crawler/search.py:23-46`, `:241-252`).
- UI default field: label "Search" with an empty value (`SearchSettings.tsx:6-10`). No built-in search terms exist.
- Refuses password, file, hidden, submit and button inputs (`search.py:246-248`).
- Submit, result and next controls are refused if their label matches the destructive-word list (`search.py:282-285`).
- Consent is required: `confirmed: Literal[True]` (`search.py:38`), plus the UI checkbox "I authorize these search inputs and result clicks..." (`SearchSettings.tsx:74-75`).
- UI warning: "Use non-sensitive examples. Settings are saved with the local report. Never enter passwords, verification codes, or personal records." (`SearchSettings.tsx:49`). The search config is saved in `config_json` (`orchestrator.py:810`).

Limits:
- max_results 1 to 50 (default 20), result pages 1 to 5 (default 3), per-step timeout 0.5 to 15 s (default 5 s) (`search.py:44-46`).
- Whole journey capped at 120 s (`search.py:159`).

How results are added:
- The search runs when the entry page (the search page URL, or the start page if blank) is rendered (`js_fetcher.py:427-435`; entry at `search.py:112`). The search page is also seeded into the queue (`orchestrator.py:421-424`).
- The runner collects result `href`s and clicks non-link options to capture the route or DOM state. It re-runs axe on each result state (`search.py:144-155`, `:160-214`).
- Discovered URLs go into the normal frontier at depth+1, under normal scope, blocklist, depth and page limits (`js_fetcher.py:461-463`; `orchestrator.py:1465-1471`, `:1561`).
- Axe findings are stored with `revealed_by="Configured search"` (`search.py:155`; `orchestrator.py:1254-1265`).
- Outcome states: completed, limited, no_results, failed (`search.py:49-54`). They are stored in `scan_search_runs` (`orchestrator.py:1255-1257`).
- Only out-of-scope main-frame navigations are blocked during search (`search.py:126-135`). No HTTP write guard applies, unlike the click probe (`server.py:3591-3596`; `docs/spa-search-scans.md:105-107`).

Tests:
- Five scenarios (delayed route, DOM-only, empty, unsafe password field, limit) (`tests/integration/test_search_journeys.py:16-80`).
- A real Vue app reaches 51 routes in public, login and login-hash modes (`tests/integration/test_vue_search.py:36-74`).

##### A4. Menus, dialogs, tabs (interaction probe; brief)

Behavior:
- On by default: `interaction_checks_enabled=True` (`orchestrator.py:263-280`), UI `skip_interaction:false` (`scanPolicy.ts:81`).
- UI label: "Click through menus, tabs and dialogs" (`copy.ts:82-86`).
- The probe clicks controls on each rendered page and re-runs axe on each new DOM state (`src/audit/analyzer/interaction/probe.py:1-21`).
- Bounds: 100 clicks, 20 per repeated shape, depth 5 (`orchestrator.py:278-280`), 120 s (`probe.py:316`).
- Never clicks controls whose names contain words such as sign out, delete, pay, submit, save, send, upload or download (`probe.py:60-110`).
- Blocks HTTP writes during automatic clicks. This is defense in depth, not a sandbox (`src/audit/analyzer/interaction/safety.py:1-6`).
- Needs axe: it is disabled for Alfa-only or static-only scans (`server.py:2914-2918`; `orchestrator.py:524`).
- Links revealed by clicks are queued (`js_fetcher.py:461-463`).

Stored results:
- A per-page ledger in `scan_interaction_runs` (`orchestrator.py:1428-1449`).
- Revealed-state markup in `page_dom_states` when rendered storage is on (`orchestrator.py:536`, `:1851-1865`).

DISCREPANCY: `README.md:313-314` says the New Scan form presents this "as an unchecked Advanced Settings option". It is on by default (`scanPolicy.ts:81`).

##### A5. What the scanner reports about pages or states it could not reach

**Method ledger** (report page)
- Heading "What this scan actually checked" and "{n} of {m} methods ran. Open a row for what it does and does not prove." (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/components/MethodCoverageLedger.tsx:63-69`).
- State chips: "Not selected", "Waiting", "Checking", "Ran", "Partly ran", "Did not run", "Not recorded" (`MethodCoverageLedger.tsx:21-29`). The result column shows "n/a" when a method did not run (`MethodCoverageLedger.tsx:115`).
- Rows, in order: "Configured search", "Browser rendering", "axe-core (AA)", "Siteimprove Alfa, ACT (Accessibility Conformance Testing)", "Image-of-text (OCR+VLM)", "Semantic review (local AI)", "Keyboard probe", "Responsive & zoom probe", "Click Through DOM States" (`server.py:3578-3735`).
- There are no ledger rows for the focus-obscured probe or the visual/motion probe (same range).
- Result strings (`server.py:3788-3823`):
  - "Not selected"
  - "Coverage not recorded for this older scan"
  - "Selected; waiting to run"
  - "{n} {unit}s {verb} so far"
  - "No images found to analyze"
  - "Selected, but no completed {verb} work recorded"
  - "{n} of {total} {unit}s {verb}"
- Interaction extras (`server.py:3740-3785`):
  - "{n} DOM states reached"
  - "{x} of {y} controls operated"
  - "{n} controls skipped as unsafe"
  - "{n} dialogs would not close, stopping those pages early"
  - "exploration limits reached on {n} pages"
  - "Selected, but no page checks were recorded"
- Search extra: "... result states checked; search stopped early or found no results. Check the search settings and limits." (`server.py:3746-3753`).
- The "Image-of-text (OCR+VLM)" row counts as enabled only when both OCR and VLM are on (`server.py:3647`). With UI defaults (OCR on, VLM off, `scanPolicy.ts:73-77`) it therefore shows "Not selected" even though OCR runs. This follows from the code; it was not observed in a running UI.

**Export coverage text** (`src/audit/exports/interaction_coverage.py`)
- Probe-stop reason labels (`interaction_coverage.py:33-39`).
- Status sentences (`interaction_coverage.py:129-153`). One begins "Click-through DOM state discovery was turned off for this scan, ..."
- Caveats (`interaction_coverage.py:156-189`), including: "Hover-only content, gestures, operating-system menus, closed shadow DOM, cross-origin embeds, and states with no observable DOM change are outside what this probe can reach and still require manual testing." and "Click-revealed findings are not yet compared across scans..."

**Failed pages**
- A page row with a null status is shown as "Load status unavailable". Other labels include "Sign-in required (HTTP 401)", "Access blocked (HTTP 403)", "Page not found (HTTP 404)" and "Website server error (HTTP {n})" (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/lib/pageLabels.ts:2-12`).
- Render labels: "Rendered in a real browser" / "Fetched without browser rendering" (`pageLabels.ts:14-18`).
- The scan detail page shows "Pages Tested" with the hint "{n} crawl errors" (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/routes/ScanDetail.tsx:337-342`).
- A failed scan shows "Why it failed: {failure_reason}" (`ScanDetail.tsx:286-289`).

**Not persisted or shown in the UI**
- Robots-skipped, out-of-scope and blocklisted counts and the `auth_wall` count are not written to the database. `_finalize_scan` writes only status, page_count and error_count (`orchestrator.py:2214-2230`), and grep finds no frontend field for them.
- The CLI prints "Skipped (robots.txt)" only (`cli.py:616-617`).
- Blocklist and exclusions are saved in `config_json` (`orchestrator.py:767-770`).
- `CrawlSummary.notes` (for example Alfa truncation or failure notes at `orchestrator.py:1347-1359`, "Axe scan disabled: bundle missing.") are never persisted or printed. Grep finds them only inside the orchestrator.
- Pre-scan "Not included" line, public: "Pages behind a login · other subdomains · other sections of the site." Login: "Pages on any other website. Nothing is uploaded; the session cookie is discarded when the scan ends." (`ScanSummaryCard.tsx:75-83`).

#### B. Data and privacy

##### B1. Where data lives and what is stored

**Locations**
- Source or CLI install:
  - `data/audit.db`, `data/blobs`, `data/logs` (`config.py:21-24`).
  - Log file `data/logs/axcess.log`, rotating 5 MB x 3 backups (`src/audit/logging.py:21-25`, `:37-48`), configured at web server startup (`server.py:788-798`).
  - The CLI does not configure the file log (grep: only the server calls `configure_logging`).
- Desktop app:
  - Sets `AUDIT_DATA_DIR=<userData>/data` plus `audit.db`, `blobs` and `logs` under it (`desktop/src/runtime.cjs:72-84`).
  - Launcher log at `<userData>/data/logs/launcher.log` (`desktop/src/main.cjs:51-53`).
  - Product name "Axcess" (`desktop/package.json:3`).
- ASSUMED, not run: Electron's default userData maps this to `~/Library/Application Support/Axcess/data/` (macOS) and `%APPDATA%/Axcess/data/` (Windows), as `docs/desktop-app.md:27-33` states.

**Stored per page** (`orchestrator.py:1495-1532`)
- Requested URL, final URL if redirected, HTTP status, title, render mode, SHA-256 of the body.
- Gzipped rendered HTML up to 2,000,000 uncompressed bytes (`orchestrator.py:1474-1492`).
- Rendered HTML is stored by default (`store_rendered_html=True` at `orchestrator.py:288-293`). UI "Don't store rendered pages" is off by default (`scanPolicy.ts:87`; `copy.ts:99-103`). CLI flag: `--no-store-rendered` (`cli.py:431-442`).

**Revealed-state markup**
- Gzipped, in `page_dom_states`, only when rendered storage is on (`orchestrator.py:536`, `:1851-1858`; `src/audit/analyzer/interaction/base.py:66-81`).

**Screenshots**
- Circled element crops per finding, up to 100 per page, stored as PNG blobs (`js_fetcher.py:48-53`, `:381-425`; `orchestrator.py:1722-1738`).
- On by default (`capture_screenshots=True` at `orchestrator.py:281-287`). CLI: `--skip-screenshots` (`cli.py:420-430`).
- The web public form has no screenshot switch. `_build_crawl_config` never sets it, so screenshots are always on for UI public scans (`server.py:2881-2929`).
- No full-page screenshot is stored. The visual probe's full screenshot goes only to the VLM (`src/audit/analyzer/visual/probe.py:235-240`), and only per-finding crops are persisted (`orchestrator.py:2097-2104`).

**Images**
- Downloaded page images are stored content-addressed at `<blobs>/<aa>/<sha256>.<ext>` (`src/audit/blob_store.py:1-7`, `:42-63`).

**Finding rows**
- Include selector, HTML snippet and failure summary (`orchestrator.py:1870-1888`).

**Storage summary**
- SQLite is not encrypted for public or local-login scans. Only protected-companion artifacts use the vault (A2). This is inferred: no crypto calls exist outside `src/audit/protected`.

##### B2. Network: what leaves the machine

**Target site**
- The crawler (static HTTP plus Chromium) and Alfa's own browser contact the target site (`orchestrator.py:1339`; `src/audit/analyzer/alfa.py:192`, `:220`, which spawns a local Node child).

**Ollama**
- Default URL `http://localhost:11434` (`config.py:44`), used for VLM, the visual probe and the semantic review (`orchestrator.py:1010-1016`, `:1037-1042`, `:1087-1092`).
- If Ollama is unreachable, those passes are skipped with a log line (`orchestrator.py:1017-1024`, `:1043-1049`, `:1093-1103`).
- Public scans do not check that the Ollama URL is loopback. It is configurable via the `AUDIT_OLLAMA_BASE_URL` env prefix (`config.py:14-16`). Only login scans enforce a literal loopback address (`server.py:3227-3257`).
- UI defaults turn VLM, semantic and visual off (`scanPolicy.ts:77`, `:84`, `:86`). The CLI turns them on by default (`cli.py:291-294`, `:319-330`, `:396-405`).
- Opening the New scan form makes one read-only `GET {ollama}/api/tags` request (2 s timeout) to list installed models. It never pulls a model (`server.py:1033-1058`). UI text: "Runs on this computer only. Nothing is uploaded, and no model is downloaded automatically." (`copy.ts:51-55`).

**Webhook**
- Env-gated by `AUDIT_WEBHOOK_URL` (`src/audit/exports/webhook.py:1-10`, `:26-33`).
- No production code calls it. Grep finds only `tests/unit/test_exports_webhook.py`. Nothing is sent even when the variable is set.

**MCP**
- `src/audit/mcp_server.py` defines read-only tool functions and starts no transport (`mcp_server.py:1-6`). Nothing imports it (grep).

**Desktop update check**
- Packaged builds only. One `GET https://api.github.com/repos/lsa-mis/axcess/releases/latest` with `User-Agent: Axcess/<version>` after the workbench loads (`desktop/src/updates.cjs:13-16`; `main.cjs:315-325`, `:396-414`, `:436`).
- Skipped when `AXCESS_DISABLE_UPDATE_CHECK=1` or when unpackaged (`main.cjs:397`).
- Windows can download and install updates through Squirrel after the user clicks "Update now" (`main.cjs:329-368`). macOS opens the DMG in the browser after the user clicks "Download" (`main.cjs:372-391`).
- DISCREPANCY: `docs/architecture.md:347-350` says the only runtime external calls are the target site and `localhost:11434`. The desktop update check is an additional call.

**Page inspector**
- The stored capture is rendered with the scanned site's URL as `<base href>`, so the viewer loads that site's CSS, fonts and images live. Scripts are blocked (`desktop/src/runtime.cjs:28-70`).

**UI assets**
- Fonts are vendored and never fetched from a CDN (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/fonts.css:1-16`).

**Access token**
- `AUDIT_ACCESS_TOKEN` is empty by default. The gate is a no-op when empty (`config.py:49-55`).
- When set, every request except `/health` and the protected APIs needs the token as `?token=`, `X-Access-Token`, Bearer, or the `aa_access` cookie (`server.py:641-684`).
- It is an ingress gate only (`docs/hosting.md:8-11`; `server.py:646-648`, `:658-664`).
- The desktop app forces an empty token (`runtime.cjs:80`) and binds to 127.0.0.1 on a random port (`main.cjs:66-77`, `:88`). `desktop_server.py` refuses non-loopback hosts (`src/audit/desktop_server.py:269-270`).
- `make serve` binds 0.0.0.0 and warns if the token is unset (`Makefile:54-64`).

**Protected egress**
- The loopback CONNECT proxy and public-address checks apply only to the login and companion browsers (`egress.py:1-12`, `:302`; `session.py:403`, `:443-454`).

##### B3. Deleting scans and data

**In the UI**
- Scans list "Delete", and a scan detail "Danger zone" with "Delete report" (`src/audit/web/frontend/src/routes/Scans.tsx:399-420`; `ScanDetail.tsx:395-416`).
- Confirmation text: "...Image blobs are kept (they may be referenced by other scans). This cannot be undone." (`Scans.tsx:410-414`) and "Shared image blobs may remain." (`ScanDetail.tsx:399-402`).

**API**
- `DELETE /api/scans/{id}` (`server.py:1493-1580`).
- Refuses running scans with "Cancel the running scan before deleting it." (`server.py:1515-1521`).
- Cascades DB rows, deletes that scan's jobs, and NULLs `images.first_seen_scan_id` (`server.py:1570-1579`).
- Blob files (images and screenshot PNGs) are never deleted. Grep finds no unlink or rmtree outside `src/audit/protected`.

**Everything else**
- No CLI delete command exists (`cli.py` command list: crawl, synthesize, export, serve, status, protected-*).
- `make clean` deletes `data/audit.db*`, `data/blobs` and `data/logs` for source installs (`Makefile:162-163`).
- UNVERIFIED: how to wipe desktop data or what uninstall removes. No code handles it. `docs/desktop-app.md:151-152` lists "database retention, and uninstall" as untested release work. Manually deleting the data folder (B1) is the only evident route.

---

#### C. Installation and getting started

##### C1. Desktop app

**Downloads**
- `DOWNLOAD_MACOS = .../releases/latest/download/Axcess-macOS-AppleSilicon.dmg` and `DOWNLOAD_WINDOWS = .../releases/latest/download/Axcess-Windows-x64-Setup.exe` (`site/build.py:31-38`). Repo: `https://github.com/lsa-mis/axcess`.
- CI builds on `macos-15` (artifact `axcess-macos-apple-silicon`) and `windows-2022` (`axcess-windows-x64`) (`.github/workflows/desktop-build.yml:25`, `:81`, `:88`, `:139`).
- Makers: zip for darwin, squirrel for win32, deb and rpm for linux (`desktop/forge.config.cjs:105-124`).
- Linux is not released as a preview (`site/build.py:1354`).

**Requirements**
- The site says "macOS on Apple Silicon (M1 and later) and Windows 10 or 11 (64-bit)" (`site/build.py:1195`). UNVERIFIED: minimum OS versions. The code does not set them. Electron is 43.4.0 (`desktop/package.json:29`).
- The bundle includes Playwright Chromium, Tesseract with English data, and Alfa using Electron's Node (`runtime.cjs:85-91`; `docs/desktop-app.md:18-22`).
- `--verify-runtime` checks axe, Chromium, Alfa, Tesseract and xlsx (`desktop_server.py:156-258`).
- Builds are unsigned and not notarized. First launch needs "Open Anyway" on macOS or "More info > Run anyway" on Windows (`site/build.py:1216-1241`; `docs/desktop-app.md:142-154`).
- UNVERIFIED: that the Windows install is per-user with no admin prompt. The site claims it (`site/build.py:1238`); it is standard Squirrel behavior but not visible in code.

**First run**
- Electron picks a free loopback port, starts the backend, and waits up to 60 s for `/health` (`main.cjs:31`, `:183-198`, `:416-431`).
- The backend applies migrations under a file lock, then serves (`desktop_server.py:108-126`, `:276-291`).
- The window loads `/app/` (`main.cjs:434`).
- A single-instance lock applies (`main.cjs:439-448`). Quitting kills the backend (`main.cjs:458-461`).
- On failure, the window shows "Axcess could not start" with log paths (`docs/desktop-app.md:56-72`; `main.cjs:275-298`).

**Ollama**
- Optional. Not installed or downloaded by Axcess (`docs/desktop-app.md:23-25`; `site/build.py:1116`, `:1244`; `README.md:283`).
- Works without Ollama: rendering, axe-core, Alfa, keyboard, focus, responsive, click-through, OCR (bundled Tesseract; the UI says "no model needed" at `copy.ts:104-108`), and the deterministic motion part of the visual probe (`orchestrator.py:512-520`).
- Needs Ollama:
  - VLM image review (default model `qwen3-vl:2b-instruct`, `config.py:45`).
  - Semantic review (models from `src/audit/rules/analyzer_models.yaml`, computed at `server.py:1068`).
  - Visual reading order, SC 1.3.2 (`orchestrator.py:512-520`).
- The UI disables those switches with reasons: "Ollama is not running on this computer." or "Install the local model with: ollama pull {model}" (`server.py:1086-1110`; `LocalAiGroup.tsx:52-76`).

##### C2. Developer install

**Requirements**
- Python >= 3.11 (`pyproject.toml:6`), Node >= 22.22.0 (`src/audit/web/frontend/package.json:6-7`), uv, Tesseract (`README.md:275-283`).

**Make targets** (verified against the Makefile)
- `make setup` = `uv sync` + `playwright install chromium` + `mkdir -p data/blobs data/logs` (`Makefile:19-23`).
- `make migrate` = yoyo apply to `data/audit.db` (`Makefile:25-27`).
- `make fetch-models` pulls `qwen3-vl:2b-instruct` and `moondream:2b` (`Makefile:32-35`). `make fetch-analyzer-models` pulls the YAML model tiers (`Makefile:37-40`).
- `make alfa-install` = npm ci in `src/audit/alfa_runner` (`Makefile:83-84`).
- `make frontend-build` (`Makefile:77-78`). Without it, `/app/` returns 503 "Frontend bundle not built..." (`server.py:2307-2313`).
- `make run` = uvicorn on 127.0.0.1:8765 with `--reload` (`Makefile:10-11`, `:48-49`). `make run-stable` runs without reload (`Makefile:51-52`).
- CLI: `audit serve` defaults to 127.0.0.1:8765 (`cli.py:763-767`).

##### C3. Stale doc claims (DISCREPANCY list for the rewrite)

**docs/user-guide.md**
- `:6` says `make setup` includes `ollama pull`. It does not (`Makefile:19-23`).
- `:10-11` says Ollama with a vision model is required. It is optional (`README.md:283`; `site/build.py:1244`).
- `:30` shows `--use-js` as a working option. It is a deprecated no-op (`cli.py:443-451`).
- `:53-54` says the defaults are "100 pages, 2 RPS/host". The UI default is 2500 pages and 8 workers (`scanPolicy.ts:66-69`). The CLI default is 500 pages (`cli.py:233-235`).
- `:55-56` describes a "Use real browser (Playwright) for every page" checkbox. It does not exist: rendering every page is the default, and the opt-out is "Fast crawl without a browser" (`copy.ts:124-128`).
- `:57` says "Start crawl". The button is "Start scan" (`copy.ts:199`).
- `:58-59` says "Stop crawl". The button is "Stop scan" (`ScanDetail.tsx:487`).

**Other docs and UI strings**
- `docs/spa-search-scans.md:39-41`: UI location and tab name are wrong (see A3).
- `copy.ts:180-181`, login fixed note: "respects robots.txt" and "1 request per second" are both inaccurate (see A1 and A2).
- `README.md:313-314`: click-through is not unchecked by default (see A4).
- `docs/protected-scans.md:272`, `:317-320`: origin verification and GET/HEAD-only scope no longer match the code (see A2).
- `docs/architecture.md:347-350`: omits the desktop update check (see B2).
- `README.md:288-289`: the clone creates `axcess`, but the next step is `cd Axcess`. This fails on case-sensitive filesystems. Inferred; not run.

## Appendix F: Maintainer facts: architecture, adding checks, lanes, safeguards, CI, releases

Source file: agent notes `maintainer.md`, copied verbatim with headings demoted.

### Maintainer facts for CONTRIBUTING.md and docs/internal/

Source: `/home/user/axcess` at HEAD `38eae99` (2026-09-23, "Merge pull request #28"), clean working tree (`git status --short` printed nothing).

How these facts were established:

- Default: verified by **reading** the cited source. No tests, lint, typecheck, builds, servers, or crawls were run. `uv run` would create a virtualenv and install packages, which AGENTS.md puts behind approval, and `node_modules` is absent in both `src/audit/web/frontend` and `desktop`.
- **RAN**: one read-only command. It rendered `site/build.py` in memory with system Python 3.11 and compared the output with the committed HTML (see section 8).
- **UNVERIFIED**: marked inline, with the reason.

---

#### 1. Architecture: crawl to report

##### 1.1 The runtime path in code

- **Entry points.** The CLI builds a `CrawlConfig` and calls `run_crawl` (`src/audit/cli.py:489-552`). The web server builds its own `CrawlConfig`: `src/audit/web/server.py:1186-1240` for the browser-only sign-in scan, and `server.py:2881-2926` for the standard scan builder.
- **Scan row.** `run_crawl` (`src/audit/crawler/orchestrator.py:368`):
  - normalizes the seed and builds the scope (`orchestrator.py:381-383`);
  - creates or adopts a scan row with `_ensure_scan` (`orchestrator.py:385`, defined at `683-747`);
  - reclaims expired leases, purges out-of-scope jobs, and seeds the queue (`orchestrator.py:386-422`).
- **Resume rules.** `_ensure_scan` always adopts a `running` row. It adopts an `interrupted` row only while jobs are still queued. It skips protected and non-resumable scans (`orchestrator.py:696-727`).
- **Analyzers are built once per crawl:**
  - axe (`orchestrator.py:467-484`) and Alfa (`485-498`);
  - keyboard (`499-503`), responsive (`506-507`), focus (`510-511`), visual (`516-521`), and interaction (`524-545`);
  - the `_LazyJs` Playwright holder (`546-560`) and the semantic analyzers (`564`).
- **Workers.** `config.workers` asyncio tasks run `_worker` (`orchestrator.py:593-595`, `1116-1139`). Each worker leases jobs from the SQLite queue with `lease_secs=120` (`orchestrator.py:1121`).
- **Per page, `_process_job` (`orchestrator.py:1146`) does this, in order:**
  1. Scope, blocklist, and robots checks (`1153-1175`).
  2. Static fetch, or a direct browser render (`1177-1238`), then escalation to Playwright (`1242-1248`). `js_eager` defaults to `True` (`orchestrator.py:152`), so `_should_escalate_to_js` returns `True` for every HTML response (`1703-1719`).
  3. Record the page with `_record_page` (`1250-1252`, defined at `1495`).
  4. Image extraction, OCR, and VLM through `process_page` (`1289-1309`).
  5. Persist axe (`1326-1331`) and Alfa (`1337-1361`).
  6. Persist keyboard (`1367`), responsive (`1378`), focus (`1388`), visual (`1397`), and interaction (`1414`).
  7. Run the semantic analyzers (`1456-1457`).
  8. Enqueue in-scope children (`1465-1471`).
- **Probes run inside the browser fetch, not in the orchestrator.** `JsFetcher.fetch` runs them in this fixed order:
  1. axe (`src/audit/crawler/js_fetcher.py:311`)
  2. keyboard (`322`)
  3. focus (`333`)
  4. visual (`343`)
  5. interaction (`359`)
  6. responsive, which is last because it resizes the viewport (`379`)
  7. element screenshots (`381-423`)
  8. search explorer (`432`)

  Results ride back on `FetchResult` fields (`src/audit/crawler/fetcher.py:48-98`).
- **Interaction findings are stored as `pipeline='axe'` rows** that carry `revealed_by`. `_persist_interaction` calls `repo.upsert_axe_violation` (`orchestrator.py:1867-1889`, rationale at `1404-1408`).
- **Semantic analyzers run on the HTML body.** The Playwright page is not passed (`page=None`, `orchestrator.py:2130-2143`), so semantic rows never get a screenshot (`orchestrator.py:2158-2162`).
- **Synthesis runs after the workers finish,** only when status is `completed`, synthesis is enabled, and at least one page was fetched (`orchestrator.py:607-623`).
  - `synthesize_findings` covers **image findings only**. `src/audit/synthesizer/*.py` never references `page_a11y_findings` (grep returned nothing).
  - Axe, probe, and semantic rows are grouped at read time by `src/audit/web/issues.py`, not by the synthesizer.
- **Finalize.** `_finalize_scan` writes status, page count, and error count (`orchestrator.py:2214-2230`).
- **Serving and exports.**
  - The API is `create_app` (`src/audit/web/server.py:600`). The unified issue projection is `issues.list_issues` (`issues.py:247-298`), built from `a11y_queries.grouped_by_rule` (`src/audit/web/a11y_queries.py:300`) and `image_findings_queries.grouped_by_remediation` (`issues.py:1015`).
  - Exports live in `src/audit/exports/` (`csv_export.py`, `json_export.py`, `jira_export.py`, `markdown_report.py`, `xlsx_export.py`, `audit_report.py`, `webhook.py`). The webhook is opt-in through `AUDIT_WEBHOOK_URL` (`exports/webhook.py:3,26`).

##### 1.2 AGENTS.md diagram (AGENTS.md:14-19) checked against code

- **Broadly accurate,** with three nuances:
  - "extraction / analysis / probes" is not a separate stage after rendering. The browser probes run inside the Playwright fetch (`js_fetcher.py:311-379`). Image extraction and semantic analysis run after the page is recorded (`orchestrator.py:1289-1457`).
  - "synthesis and issue grouping" are two different things:
    - Synthesis persists image findings (`orchestrator.py:616`).
    - Issue grouping is a read-time projection with nothing persisted (`issues.py:247-298`).
  - Exports read the database at request time. They are not a pipeline stage.
- **The AGENTS.md pipeline table (AGENTS.md:30-35) omits three row sources** that exist in code:
  - `alfa` (Siteimprove Alfa, opt-in, `orchestrator.py:213`);
  - `protected_image` (migration `0012_protected_image_pipeline.sql:15-18`);
  - interaction-probe rows, stored as `axe` (see 1.1).
- **AGENTS.md:75 says the MCP conversation is "not implemented yet."** A read-only function module exists, but no transport and no chat (section 9).

##### 1.3 Database

- **Migrations.** `src/audit/db/migrations/` holds 29 forward and 29 rollback `.sql` files, `0001` through `0029` (58 files, per `ls`).
  - Runtime apply: `make migrate` runs `$(PY) yoyo apply --database sqlite:///$(DB) --batch $(MIGRATIONS)` (`Makefile:25-27`). The desktop sidecar applies migrations through yoyo at startup (`src/audit/desktop_server.py:24,123-126`).
  - Tests do not use yoyo. `_apply_migrations` runs every forward `*.sql` file in filename order and skips `*.rollback.sql` (`tests/conftest.py:81-93`).
- **Tables and the migration that creates each:**

  | Migration | Tables |
  | --- | --- |
  | `0001_initial_schema.sql` | `scans` (4), `pages` (17), `images` (31), `page_images` (45), `analyses` (60), `findings` (74), `finding_history` (93), `jobs` (106) |
  | `0002` | `page_a11y_findings` (line 16) |
  | `0009` | `evaluation_reports` (7), `manual_check_results` (27), `manual_check_evidence` (41) |
  | `0011` | `protected_scans` (7), `protected_agent_enrollments` (51), `protected_audit_events` (72), `protected_artifacts` (85) |
  | `0019` | `a11y_finding_history` (8) |
  | `0025` | `scan_search_runs` (2) |
  | `0026` | `scan_interaction_runs` (13) |
  | `0028` | `page_dom_states` (21) |

- **Pipeline discriminator.** The column is `page_a11y_findings.pipeline TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'axe'`. The current CHECK allows these values (`0012_protected_image_pipeline.sql:13-18`):

  `'axe', 'semantic', 'keyboard', 'responsive', 'focus', 'visual', 'alfa', 'protected_image'`

  Each value was added by a migration:

  | Migration | Change |
  | --- | --- |
  | `0003` | Creates the column with `('axe','semantic')` and adds `criterion_sc` (lines 20-28) |
  | `0004` | Adds `keyboard` (38-39) |
  | `0005` | Adds `responsive` (23-24) |
  | `0006` | Adds `focus` (10-11) |
  | `0007` | Adds `visual` (10-11) |
  | `0010` | Adds `alfa` (20-21) |
  | `0012` | Adds `protected_image` |

- **Widening the CHECK.** SQLite cannot alter a CHECK in place. Every widening follows the same steps (`0004_keyboard_pipeline.sql:14-48`):
  1. Drop the `idx_a11y_pipeline` index.
  2. Copy `pipeline` into `_pipeline_tmp`.
  3. Drop the column and re-add it with the wider CHECK.
  4. Restore the values, drop the temp column, and recreate the index.
- **`"image"` is not a DB pipeline value.** It is the `IssueRow.pipeline` for rows from the `findings` table (`issues.py:1060`).
- **The dedupe key ignores `pipeline`.** The key is `UNIQUE (page_id, rule_id, target_hash)` (`0002_page_a11y_findings.sql:45`), and the upsert `ON CONFLICT` does not update `pipeline` (`src/audit/db/repo.py:668-683`).
  - A new check must use rule_ids that no other pipeline uses. The focus probe prefixes its rule_ids for this reason (`src/audit/analyzer/focus/base.py:14-17`).
  - Semantic rows use `rule_id = "semantic:<sc>"` (`repo.py:883`).
- **Per-scan method coverage counters.**
  - `scans.semantic_pages_analyzed`, `keyboard_pages_probed`, and `responsive_pages_probed` (`0020_method_coverage.sql:8-13`) are written through `repo.increment_scan_method_coverage`. Its `method` parameter is `Literal["semantic","keyboard","responsive"]` (`repo.py:1013`, column map `1026-1030`).
  - Axe and Alfa have their own counters (`repo.py:955`, `981`).
  - **Focus and visual have no persisted coverage counter.** `_persist_focus` (`orchestrator.py:2060-2086`) and `_persist_visual` (`2089-2114`) do not call it.
  - The server's "methods used" list, `_methods_used` (`server.py:3550`), has rows for `search`, `rendered`, `axe`, `alfa`, `image`, `semantic`, `keyboard`, `responsive`, and `interaction` (`server.py:3580-3706`). It has no `focus` or `visual` row.
- **Config snapshot.** `scans.config_json` is written by `config_json_for_scan` (`orchestrator.py:749-818`). It records the pipeline on/off flags. It does **not** record:
  - `semantic_criteria`;
  - any model name;
  - `vlm_prompt_name`;
  - OCR thresholds;
  - `keyboard_probe_max_focusable`.

#### 2. How to add a new check

##### 2.1 Example commits

- **No granular example commit exists.** The repo has 188 commits. The root commit `240089b` (2026-08-31) is a bulk import that added every migration and analyzer at once.
  - `git log --diff-filter=A` on `0004`-`0007` and on `analyzer/focus` and `analyzer/visual` returns only `240089b`.
  - Later analyzer commits touch only the interaction probe (for example `98c15e2` "Capture the markup of states that reveal a new finding" and `2b744ea` "Photograph a revealed element while its state is still open").
  - **UNVERIFIED:** the original per-pipeline development history is not in this repository.
- **Substitute: tracing the focus probe.** The checklist in 2.2 comes from following the smallest browser probe through the tree. The grep for `FocusProbe`, `upsert_focus_finding`, `focus_checks_enabled`, `skip_focus`, and `"focus"` matched 47 files. Protected-mode and migration files are excluded from the list below.

##### 2.2 Checklist: new browser probe (new pipeline value `X`)

**Analyzer and browser fetch**

1. **Analyzer package** `src/audit/analyzer/<x>/` with `__init__.py`, `base.py`, and `probe.py`.
   - Follow the pattern in `focus/base.py:26-75`: a frozen dataclass with `rule_id`, `target_selector`, `html_snippet`, a `target_hash` property, and `to_repo_kwargs()` that returns `"pipeline": "<x>"` and `criterion_sc`.
   - Rule ids must be prefixed and globally unique (1.3).
   - The probe must never raise. The fetch code relies on this (`js_fetcher.py:312-315`).
2. **Browser fetch.**
   - Add a `FetchResult` field (pattern: `fetcher.py:57-63`).
   - Add a `JsFetcher.__init__` parameter (pattern: `js_fetcher.py:129-133`, `159-165`).
   - Add the run step in `fetch()` in the correct order. Anything that mutates the viewport goes last, like responsive (`js_fetcher.py:323-326`, `373-379`).
   - Add the findings to the screenshot candidate list (`js_fetcher.py:392-399`) and to the returned `FetchResult` (`js_fetcher.py:444-448`).

**Crawl config and orchestrator**

3. **`CrawlConfig` flag** (pattern: `focus_checks_enabled`, `orchestrator.py:256`).
   - Construct the probe in `run_crawl` (`orchestrator.py:510-511`).
   - Thread it through `_LazyJs.__init__` (`orchestrator.py:885`) and the `_LazyJs(...)` call (`546-560`).
4. **Persistence helper.** Write `_persist_<x>` decorated with `@_batched_writes`, following `_persist_focus` (`orchestrator.py:2060-2086`). Call it in `_process_job` gated on `render_mode == "js"` and the flag (`orchestrator.py:1386-1393`).
5. **Repo function.** Write `upsert_<x>_finding` that delegates to `upsert_keyboard_finding(pipeline=...)` (patterns: `repo.py:758-802` for focus, `804-848` for visual).
6. **Migration.** Write `00NN_<x>_pipeline.sql` plus `.rollback.sql` that widens the CHECK with the column dance (template: `0012_protected_image_pipeline.sql`).
   - For a coverage counter, also add a `scans` column (pattern: `0020`) and extend the `Literal` and column map in `repo.increment_scan_method_coverage` (`repo.py:1013,1026-1030`).
7. **Scan snapshot.** Add the flag to `config_json_for_scan` (`orchestrator.py:789-793`).

**Entry points**

   - CLI option `--skip-<x>` (pattern: `cli.py:386-389`, wired at `cli.py:524`).

   - Add `<x>` to the tuple in `_rule_meta_for` (`issues.py:469`). Otherwise the YAML lookup falls through to `image_findings` (`issues.py:483`).
   - Add it to the `_pages_for_issue` tuple (`issues.py:511-520`).

##### 2.3 Axe-like engine or new axe rules

- **New axe rules need no registration.** They come from the vendored `src/audit/web/static/axe.min.js` (v4.10.2 per its header), filtered by level tags (`analyzer/axe.py:58,72`).
  - To give a rule a card, add it under `axe_rules:` in `audit_report.yaml` (starts at line 34), keyed by rule_id.
  - "Rules NOT listed here still appear ... flagged human review needed" (`audit_report.yaml:6-9`).
- **A second engine should follow the Alfa template:**
  - analyzer `analyzer/alfa.py` and `alfa_evidence.py`;
  - its own upsert (`repo.py:1038`) and counters (`repo.py:981`);
  - migration `0010` with the `engine_outcome` CHECK (`0010_alfa_scan_engine.sql:10`);
  - outcome subgrouping (`a11y_queries.py:359-363`);
  - an explicit issues branch (`issues.py:881-945`);
  - the orchestrator hook (`orchestrator.py:1337-1361`).

##### 2.4 Adding a semantic analyzer

Instructions live in `src/audit/analyzer/semantic/registry.py:9-11` and `src/audit/analyzer/semantic/prompts/README.md:39-49`. Verified steps:

1. **Class.** Write `src/audit/analyzer/semantic/analyzers/sc_<n>_<n>_<n>.py` implementing the `SemanticAnalyzer` protocol (`semantic/base.py:89`).
   - Pattern: `sc_2_4_6.py:28-56`. It sets `criterion_sc`, accepts `model=`, loads the prompt through `importlib.resources`, and sets `self.prompt_version`.
   - It fails soft: an empty list and a logged warning (`sc_2_4_6.py:8-11`).
   - Export the class from `analyzers/__init__.py`.
2. **Register it.** Add the `{sc: cls}` row in `_REGISTRY` (`registry.py:34-39`). Today that holds `1.2.1`, `2.4.4`, `2.4.6`, and `3.3.2`.
3. **Prompt.** Add `semantic/prompts/sc_<n>_<n>_<n>_<slug>.txt` (naming rule at `prompts/README.md:7-10`) with an `{elements}` placeholder (filled by `.format(elements=...)`, `sc_2_4_6.py:82`).
4. **Extraction.** Reuse or add a function in `semantic/extractor.py`: `extract_links` (60), `extract_headings` (273), `extract_form_fields` (378), `extract_media` (508).
5. **Report card.** Add one under `semantic_criteria:` in `audit_report.yaml` (lookup at `issues.py:726-728` and `463-468`).
   - **Current gap:** `1.2.1`, `2.4.6`, and `3.3.2` have no card. `grep` finds them only in `wcag_coverage.yaml`.
   - Without a card, the title falls back to `"WCAG SC {sc} (LLM-detected)"` (`issues.py:771`) with no what/why/fix text.
6. **Model pick.** Adding one in `rules/analyzer_models.yaml` `criteria:` is optional. `get_pick` falls back to `defaults[kind]` (`model_registry.py:52-83`).
7. **Default criteria.** The default list is `CrawlConfig.semantic_criteria` (`orchestrator.py:224-236`), **not `src/audit/config.py`** as `prompts/README.md:47-48` says. `config.py` has no semantic settings.
   - The default already lists 11 SCs. The 7 without an analyzer log `semantic.unknown_criterion` on every crawl (`registry.py:76-83`).
8. **Tests and fixtures.**
   - Unit test: `tests/unit/test_semantic_sc_<n>.py` (existing: `test_semantic_sc_1_2_1.py`, `_2_4_4`, `_2_4_6`, `_3_3_2`).
   - Fixture pages: `tests/fixtures/site/sc_<n>/` (existing: `sc_1_2_1`, `sc_2_4_4`, `sc_2_4_6`, `sc_3_3_2`).
   - Registry behavior test: `tests/unit/test_semantic_runner_and_registry.py`.
9. **Coverage surfaces.**
   - `wcag_coverage.yaml`.
   - `coverage_status.py`: the ROADMAP entry, plus the hardcoded "Four per-criterion analyzers are registered" (`coverage_status.py:117`) and the `scs` string (`112-115`).
   - The server's semantic method description (`server.py:3666-3670`), which names the covered topics.
10. **Lane.** No action needed. Semantic rows are always `expert_review` (section 4).

Stale or at-risk points for semantic analyzers:

- **`prompts/README.md:31-37` is stale.** It says the semantic runner stores the prompt hash in `analyses.model_versions_json`.
  - The analyzer computes `prompt_version` (`sc_2_4_4.py:72`), but `upsert_semantic_finding` has no prompt or model column (`repo.py:850-927`).
  - No migration adds `prompt_version` or `model_version` to `page_a11y_findings`. The grep matched only `0001_initial_schema.sql:69-70`, which is `analyses`.
  - Semantic findings therefore carry no record of which model or prompt produced them.
- **UNVERIFIED packaging risk:** the desktop PyInstaller spec may leave out the semantic prompts.
  - `desktop/backend.spec:10-18` bundles `analyzer/vlm/prompts`. It does not bundle `analyzer/semantic/prompts`.
  - Analyzers read their prompt when they are constructed (`sc_2_4_6.py:37-38,55`).
  - A packaged app with semantic enabled and Ollama healthy may fail to build analyzers. The UI defaults to `skip_semantic: true` (`frontend/src/components/newScan/scanPolicy.ts:84`).
  - Not tested because no packaged build was made.
